182	pub const DerivedConfig = struct {
183	    arena: ArenaAllocator,
184	
185	    palette: terminalpkg.color.Palette,
186	    image_storage_limit: usize,
187	    cursor_style: terminalpkg.CursorStyle,
188	    cursor_blink: ?bool,
189	    cursor_color: ?configpkg.Config.TerminalColor,
190	    foreground: configpkg.Config.Color,
191	    background: configpkg.Config.Color,
192	    osc_color_report_format: configpkg.Config.OSCColorReportFormat,
193	    clipboard_write: configpkg.ClipboardAccess,
194	    clipboard_write_limit: usize,
195	    enquiry_response: []const u8,
196	    xt_checksum_report: bool,
197	    xt_checksum_extension: terminalpkg.xt_checksum.Flags,
198	    conditional_state: configpkg.ConditionalState,
199	
200	    pub fn init(
201	        alloc_gpa: Allocator,
202	        config: *const configpkg.Config,
203	    ) !DerivedConfig {
204	        var arena = ArenaAllocator.init(alloc_gpa);
205	        errdefer arena.deinit();
206	        const alloc = arena.allocator();
207	
208	        const palette: terminalpkg.color.Palette = palette: {
209	            if (config.@"palette-generate") generate: {
210	                if (config.palette.mask.findFirstSet() == null) {
211	                    // If the user didn't set any values manually, then
212	                    // we're using the default palette and we don't need
213	                    // to apply the generation code to it.
214	                    break :generate;
215	                }
216	
217	                break :palette terminalpkg.color.generate256Color(config.palette.value, config.palette.mask, config.background.toTerminalRGB(), config.foreground.toTerminalRGB(), config.@"palette-harmonious");
218	            }
219	
220	            break :palette config.palette.value;
221	        };
222	
223	        return .{
224	            .palette = palette,
225	            .image_storage_limit = config.@"image-storage-limit",
226	            .cursor_style = config.@"cursor-style",
227	            .cursor_blink = config.@"cursor-style-blink",
228	            .cursor_color = config.@"cursor-color",
229	            .foreground = config.foreground,
230	            .background = config.background,
231	            .osc_color_report_format = config.@"osc-color-report-format",
232	            .clipboard_write = config.@"clipboard-write",
233	            .clipboard_write_limit = config.@"clipboard-write-limit-bytes".value,
234	            .enquiry_response = try alloc.dupe(u8, config.@"enquiry-response"),
235	            .xt_checksum_report = config.@"vt-xt-checksum-report",
236	            .xt_checksum_extension = xtChecksumFlags(config.@"vt-xt-checksum-extension"),
237	            .conditional_state = config._conditional_state,
238	
239	            // This has to be last so that we copy AFTER the arena allocations
240	            // above happen (Zig assigns in order).
241	            .arena = arena,
242	        };
243	    }
244	
245	    pub fn deinit(self: *DerivedConfig) void {
246	        self.arena.deinit();
247	    }
248	};
254	pub fn init(self: *Termio, alloc: Allocator, opts: termio.Options) !void {
255	    // The default terminal modes based on our config.
256	    const default_modes: terminalpkg.ModePacked = modes: {
257	        var modes: terminalpkg.ModePacked = .{};
258	
259	        // Setup our initial grapheme cluster support if enabled. We use a
260	        // switch to ensure we get a compiler error if more cases are added.
261	        switch (opts.full_config.@"grapheme-width-method") {
262	            .unicode => modes.grapheme_cluster = true,
263	            .legacy => {},
264	        }
265	
266	        // Set default cursor blink settings
267	        modes.cursor_blinking = opts.config.cursor_blink orelse true;
268	
269	        break :modes modes;
270	    };
271	
272	    // Create our terminal
273	    var term = try terminalpkg.Terminal.init(global.io(), alloc, opts: {
274	        const grid_size = opts.size.grid();
275	        break :opts .{
276	            .cols = grid_size.columns,
277	            .rows = grid_size.rows,
278	            .max_scrollback_bytes = opts.full_config.@"scrollback-limit-bytes".optional(),
279	            .max_scrollback_lines = opts.full_config.@"scrollback-limit-lines".optional(),
280	            .default_modes = default_modes,
281	            .default_cursor_style = opts.config.cursor_style,
282	            .default_cursor_blink = opts.config.cursor_blink,
283	            .default_xt_checksum = opts.config.xt_checksum_extension,
284	            .colors = .{
285	                .background = .init(opts.config.background.toTerminalRGB()),
286	                .foreground = .init(opts.config.foreground.toTerminalRGB()),
287	                .cursor = cursor: {
288	                    const color = opts.config.cursor_color orelse break :cursor .unset;
289	                    const rgb = color.toTerminalRGB() orelse break :cursor .unset;
290	                    break :cursor .init(rgb);
291	                },
292	                .palette = .default,
293	            },
294	            .kitty_image_storage_limit = opts.config.image_storage_limit,
295	            .kitty_image_loading_limits = .allWithTempDir(global.tmpDirPath()),
296	        };
297	    });
298	    errdefer term.deinit(alloc);
299	
300	    // The default palette may be an allocator-owned copy, so it is set
301	    // once the terminal owns its memory and can release it on deinit.
302	    try term.colors.palette.changeDefault(alloc, opts.config.palette);
303	
304	    // Setup our terminal size in pixels for certain requests.
305	    term.width_px = term.cols * opts.size.cell.width;
306	    term.height_px = term.rows * opts.size.cell.height;
307	
308	    // Setup our backend.
309	    var backend = opts.backend;
310	    backend.initTerminal(&term);
311	
312	    // Create our stream handler. This points to memory in self so it
313	    // isn't safe to use until self.* is set.
314	    const handler: StreamHandler = .{
315	        .alloc = alloc,
316	        .termio_mailbox = &self.mailbox,
317	        .surface_mailbox = opts.surface_mailbox,
318	        .renderer_state = opts.renderer_state,
319	        .renderer_wakeup = opts.renderer_wakeup,
320	        .renderer_mailbox = opts.renderer_mailbox,
321	        .size = &self.size,
322	        .terminal = &self.terminal,
323	        .osc_color_report_format = opts.config.osc_color_report_format,
324	        .clipboard_write = opts.config.clipboard_write,
325	        .clipboard_write_limit = opts.config.clipboard_write_limit,
326	        .enquiry_response = opts.config.enquiry_response,
327	        .xt_checksum_report = opts.config.xt_checksum_report,
328	    };
329	
330	    const thread_enter_state = try ThreadEnterState.create(
331	        alloc,
332	        opts.full_config,
333	    );
334	
335	    self.* = .{
336	        .alloc = alloc,
337	        .terminal = term,
338	        .config = opts.config,
339	        .renderer_state = opts.renderer_state,
340	        .renderer_wakeup = opts.renderer_wakeup,
341	        .renderer_mailbox = opts.renderer_mailbox,
342	        .surface_mailbox = opts.surface_mailbox,
343	        .size = opts.size,
344	        .backend = backend,
345	        .mailbox = opts.mailbox,
346	        .terminal_stream = .init(.{
347	            .allocator = alloc,
348	            .handler = handler,
349	        }),
350	        .thread_enter_state = thread_enter_state,
351	    };
352	}
354	pub fn deinit(self: *Termio) void {
355	    self.backend.deinit();
356	    self.terminal.deinit(self.alloc);
357	    self.config.deinit();
358	    self.mailbox.deinit(self.alloc);
359	
360	    // Clear any StreamHandler state
361	    self.terminal_stream.deinit();
362	
363	    // Clear any initial state if we have it
364	    if (self.thread_enter_state) |v| v.destroy();
365	}
427	pub fn threadExit(self: *Termio, data: *ThreadData) void {
428	    self.backend.threadExit(data);
429	}
431	/// Send a message to the mailbox. Depending on the mailbox type in use
432	/// this may process now or it may just enqueue and process later.
433	///
434	/// This will also notify the mailbox thread to process the message. If
435	/// you're sending a lot of messages, it may be more efficient to use
436	/// the mailbox directly and then call notify separately.
437	pub fn queueMessage(
438	    self: *Termio,
439	    msg: termio.Message,
440	    mutex: MutexState,
441	) void {
442	    self.mailbox.send(msg, switch (mutex) {
443	        .locked => self.renderer_state.mutex,
444	        .unlocked => null,
445	    });
446	    self.mailbox.notify();
447	}
465	/// Update the configuration.
466	pub fn changeConfig(self: *Termio, td: *ThreadData, config: *DerivedConfig) !void {
467	    // The remainder of this function is modifying terminal state or
468	    // the read thread data, all of which requires holding the renderer
469	    // state lock.
470	    self.renderer_state.mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
471	    defer self.renderer_state.mutex.unlock(global.io());
472	
473	    // Deinit our old config. We do this in the lock because the
474	    // stream handler may be referencing the old config (i.e. enquiry resp)
475	    self.config.deinit();
476	    self.config = config.*;
477	
478	    // Update our stream handler. The stream handler uses the same
479	    // renderer mutex so this is safe to do despite being executed
480	    // from another thread.
481	    self.terminal_stream.handler.changeConfig(&self.config);
482	    td.backend.changeConfig(&self.config);
483	
484	    // Update the configuration that we know about.
485	    //
486	    // Specific things we don't update:
487	    //   - command, working-directory: we never restart the underlying
488	    //   process so we don't care or need to know about these.
489	
490	    // Update the default palette. A config change must not fail here, so
491	    // if we can't allocate the copy of the configured palette we fall back
492	    // to the built-in default, which never allocates.
493	    self.terminal.colors.palette.changeDefault(
494	        self.alloc,
495	        config.palette,
496	    ) catch |err| {
497	        log.warn("error changing default palette, using built-in default err={}", .{err});
498	        self.terminal.colors.palette.resetDefault(self.alloc);
499	    };
500	    self.terminal.flags.dirty.palette = true;
501	
502	    // Update all our other colors
503	    self.terminal.colors.background.default = config.background.toTerminalRGB();
504	    self.terminal.colors.foreground.default = config.foreground.toTerminalRGB();
505	    self.terminal.colors.cursor.default = cursor: {
506	        const color = config.cursor_color orelse break :cursor null;
507	        break :cursor color.toTerminalRGB() orelse break :cursor null;
508	    };
509	
510	    // Set the image limits
511	    self.terminal.setKittyGraphicsSizeLimit(self.alloc, config.image_storage_limit);
512	    self.terminal.setKittyGraphicsLoadingLimits(.allWithTempDir(global.tmpDirPath()));
513	
514	    // The config could have changed any of our colors so update mode 2031.
515	    // This is written directly rather than queued as a color_scheme_report
516	    // message because we're on the io thread, the only reader of our
517	    // mailbox: if the mailbox were full, queueing would wait forever.
518	    self.colorSchemeReportLocked(td, false) catch |err| {
519	        log.warn("error sending color scheme report err={}", .{err});
520	    };
521	}
524	pub fn resize(
525	    self: *Termio,
526	    td: *ThreadData,
527	    size: renderer.Size,
528	) !void {
529	    self.size = size;
530	    const grid_size = size.grid();
531	
532	    // Enter the critical area that we want to keep small
533	    {
534	        self.renderer_state.mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
535	        defer self.renderer_state.mutex.unlock(global.io());
536	
537	        // Resizing the pty can immediately wake the shell with SIGWINCH.
538	        // Hold the same lock used by processOutput before notifying it, so
539	        // its redraw cannot be parsed against the old terminal dimensions.
540	        try self.backend.resize(grid_size, size.terminal());
541	
542	        // Update the size of our terminal state
543	        try self.terminal.resize(
544	            self.alloc,
545	            .{
546	                .cols = grid_size.columns,
547	                .rows = grid_size.rows,
548	                .cell_size_px = .{
549	                    .width = self.size.cell.width,
550	                    .height = self.size.cell.height,
551	                },
552	            },
553	        );
554	
555	        // If we have size reporting enabled we need to send a report.
556	        if (self.terminal.modes.get(.in_band_size_reports)) {
557	            try self.sizeReportLocked(td, .mode_2048);
558	        }
559	    }
560	
561	    // Mail the renderer so that it can update the GPU and re-render
562	    _ = self.renderer_mailbox.push(global.io(), .{ .resize = size }, .{ .forever = {} });
563	    self.renderer_wakeup.notify() catch {};
564	}
736	    // If we have an inspector, we enter SLOW MODE because we need to
737	    // process a byte at a time alternating between the inspector handler
738	    // and the termio handler. This is very slow compared to our optimizations
739	    // below but at least users only pay for it if they're using the inspector.
740	    if (self.renderer_state.inspector) |insp| {
741	        for (buf, 0..) |byte, i| {
742	            insp.recordPtyRead(
743	                self.alloc,
744	                &self.terminal,
745	                buf[i .. i + 1],
746	            ) catch |err| {
747	                log.err("error recording pty read in inspector err={}", .{err});
748	            };
749	
750	            self.terminal_stream.next(byte);
751	        }
752	    } else {
753	        self.terminal_stream.nextSlice(buf);
754	    }
848	    pub fn deinit(self: *ThreadData) void {
849	        self.backend.deinit(self.alloc);
850	        self.* = undefined;
851	    }
==== src/apprt/embedded.zig ====
201	    /// See CoreApp.keyEvent.
202	    pub fn keyEvent(
203	        self: *App,
204	        target: KeyTarget,
205	        event: KeyEvent,
206	    ) !bool {
207	        // Convert our C key event into a Zig one.
208	        const input_event: input.KeyEvent = event.core() orelse
209	            return false;
210	
211	        // Invoke the core Ghostty logic to handle this input.
212	        const effect: CoreSurface.InputEffect = switch (target) {
213	            .app => if (self.core_app.keyEvent(
214	                self,
215	                input_event,
216	            )) .consumed else .ignored,
217	
218	            .surface => |surface| try surface.core_surface.keyCallback(
219	                input_event,
220	            ),
221	        };
222	
223	        return switch (effect) {
224	            .closed => true,
225	            .ignored => false,
226	            .consumed => true,
227	        };
228	    }
303	    pub fn performAction(
304	        self: *App,
305	        target: apprt.Target,
306	        comptime action: apprt.Action.Key,
307	        value: apprt.Action.Value(action),
308	    ) !bool {
309	        // Special case certain actions before they are sent to the
310	        // embedded apprt.
311	        self.performPreAction(target, action, value);
312	
313	        log.debug("dispatching action target={t} action={} value={any}", .{
314	            target,
315	            action,
316	            value,
317	        });
318	        return self.opts.action(
319	            self,
320	            target.cval(),
321	            @unionInit(apprt.Action, @tagName(action), value).cval(),
322	        );
323	    }
447	pub const Surface = struct {
448	    app: *App,
449	    platform: Platform,
450	    userdata: ?*anyopaque = null,
451	    core_surface: CoreSurface,
452	    content_scale: apprt.ContentScale,
453	    size: apprt.SurfaceSize,
454	    cursor_pos: apprt.CursorPos,
455	    inspector: ?*Inspector = null,
617	        try self.core_surface.init(
618	            app.core_app.alloc,
619	            &config,
620	            app.core_app,
621	            app,
622	            self,
623	        );
624	        errdefer self.core_surface.deinit();
634	    pub fn deinit(self: *Surface) void {
635	        // Shut down our inspector
636	        self.freeInspector();
637	
638	        // Free our title
639	        if (self.title) |v| self.app.core_app.alloc.free(v);
640	
641	        // Remove ourselves from the list of known surfaces in the app.
642	        self.app.core_app.deleteSurface(self);
643	
644	        // Clean up our core surface so that all the rendering and IO stop.
645	        self.core_surface.deinit();
646	    }
651	    pub fn initInspector(self: *Surface) !*Inspector {
652	        if (self.inspector) |v| return v;
653	
654	        const alloc = self.app.core_app.alloc;
655	        const inspector = try alloc.create(Inspector);
656	        errdefer alloc.destroy(inspector);
657	        inspector.* = try .init(self);
658	        self.inspector = inspector;
659	        return inspector;
660	    }
661	
662	    pub fn freeInspector(self: *Surface) void {
663	        if (self.inspector) |v| {
664	            v.deinit();
665	            self.app.core_app.alloc.destroy(v);
666	            self.inspector = null;
667	        }
668	    }
678	    pub fn close(self: *const Surface, process_alive: bool) void {
679	        const func = self.app.opts.close_surface orelse {
680	            log.info("runtime embedder does not support closing a surface", .{});
681	            return;
682	        };
683	
684	        func(self.userdata, process_alive);
685	    }
1026	    pub fn updateSize(self: *Surface, width: u32, height: u32) void {
1027	        // Runtimes sometimes generate superfluous resize events even
1028	        // if the size did not actually change (SwiftUI). We check
1029	        // that the size actually changed from what we last recorded
1030	        // since resizes are expensive.
1031	        if (self.size.width == width and self.size.height == height) return;
1032	
1033	        self.size = .{
1034	            .width = width,
1035	            .height = height,
1036	        };
1037	
1038	        // Call the primary callback.
1039	        self.core_surface.sizeCallback(self.size) catch |err| {
1040	            log.err("error in size callback err={}", .{err});
1041	            return;
1042	        };
1043	    }
1052	    pub fn mouseButtonCallback(
1053	        self: *Surface,
1054	        action: input.MouseButtonState,
1055	        button: input.MouseButton,
1056	        mods: input.Mods,
1057	    ) bool {
1058	        return self.core_surface.mouseButtonCallback(action, button, mods) catch |err| {
1059	            log.err("error in mouse button callback err={}", .{err});
1060	            return false;
1061	        };
1062	    }
1131	    pub fn textCallback(self: *Surface, text: []const u8) void {
1132	        _ = self.core_surface.textCallback(text) catch |err| {
1133	            log.err("error in key callback err={}", .{err});
1134	            return;
1135	        };
1136	    }
1284	        const ig_ctx = cimgui.c.ImGui_CreateContext(null) orelse return error.OutOfMemory;
1285	        errdefer cimgui.c.ImGui_DestroyContext(ig_ctx);
1286	        cimgui.c.ImGui_SetCurrentContext(ig_ctx);
1287	        const io: *cimgui.c.ImGuiIO = cimgui.c.ImGui_GetIO();
1288	        io.BackendPlatformName = "ghostty_embedded";
1289	
1290	        // Setup our core inspector
1291	        CoreInspector.setup();
1292	        surface.core_surface.activateInspector() catch |err| {
1293	            log.err("failed to activate inspector err={}", .{err});
1294	        };
1295	
1296	        return .{
1297	            .surface = surface,
1298	            .ig_ctx = ig_ctx,
1299	        };
1300	    }

657	        /// The apprt Surface.
658	        rt_surface: ApprtSurface = undefined,
659	
660	        /// The core surface backing this GTK surface.
661	        ///
662	        /// This starts out null and unrealized and is initialized eagerly
663	        /// when we get our first resize event, since we don't know what
664	        /// size GTK will allocate for this widget beforehand. This will
665	        /// then be realized when the widget itself is realized.
666	        core_surface: ?*CoreSurface = null,
1445	        // Invoke the core Ghostty logic to handle this input.
1446	        const surface = priv.core_surface orelse return false;
1447	        const effect = surface.keyCallback(.{
1448	            .action = action,
1449	            .key = physical_key,
1450	            .mods = mods,
1451	            .consumed_mods = consumed_mods,
1452	            .composing = priv.im_composing,
1453	            .utf8 = priv.im_buf[0..priv.im_len],
1454	            .unshifted_codepoint = keyval_unicode_unshifted,
1455	        }) catch |err| {
1456	            log.err("error in key callback err={}", .{err});
1457	            return false;
1458	        };
1459	
1460	        switch (effect) {
1461	            .closed => return true,
1462	            .ignored => {},
1463	            .consumed => if (action == .press or action == .repeat) {
1464	                // If we were in the composing state then we reset our context.
1465	                // We do NOT want to reset if we're not in the composing state
1466	                // because there is other IME state that we want to preserve,
1467	                // such as quotation mark ordering for Chinese input.
1468	                if (priv.im_composing) {
1469	                    priv.im_context.as(gtk.IMContext).reset();
1470	                    surface.preeditCallback(null) catch {};
1471	                }
1472	
1473	                // Bell stops ringing when any key is pressed that is used by
1474	                // the core in any way.
1475	                self.setBellRinging(false);
1476	
1477	                return true;
1478	            },
1479	        }
1480	
1481	        return false;
1482	    }

1527	        self: *Self,
1528	        data: apprt.surface.Message.ChildExited,

1532	        priv.child_exited = true;

1534	            properties.@"child-exited".impl.param_spec,

1963	    fn finalize(self: *Self) callconv(.c) void {
1964	        const alloc = Application.default().allocator();
1965	        const priv = self.private();
1966	        if (priv.core_surface) |v| {
1967	            // Remove ourselves from the list of known surfaces in the app.
1968	            // We do this before deinit in case a callback triggers
1969	            // searching for this surface.
1970	            Application.default().core().deleteSurface(self.rt());
1971	
1972	            // NOTE: We must deinit the surface in the finalize call and NOT
1973	            // the dispose call because the inspector widget relies on this
1974	            // behavior with a weakRef to properly deactivate.
1975	
1976	            // Deinit the surface
1977	            v.deinit();
1978	            alloc.destroy(v);
1979	
1980	            priv.core_surface = null;
1981	        }
2923	    fn gcMouseDown(
2924	        gesture: *gtk.GestureClick,
2925	        _: c_int,
2926	        x: f64,
2927	        y: f64,
2928	        self: *Self,
2929	    ) callconv(.c) void {
2930	        const event = gesture.as(gtk.EventController).getCurrentEvent() orelse return;
2931	
2932	        // Bell stops ringing if any mouse button is pressed.
2933	        self.setBellRinging(false);
2934	
2935	        // Get our surface. If we don't have one, ignore this.
2936	        const priv = self.private();
2937	        const core_surface = priv.core_surface orelse return;
2938	
2939	        // If we don't have focus, grab it.
2940	        const widget = priv.render_surface.as(gtk.Widget);
2941	        const had_focus = widget.hasFocus() != 0;
2942	        if (!had_focus) {
2943	            _ = widget.grabFocus();
2944	        }
2945	
2946	        // Report the event
2947	        const button = translateMouseButton(gesture.as(gtk.GestureSingle).getCurrentButton());
2948	
2949	        // If this click is only transitioning split focus, suppress it so
2950	        // it doesn't get forwarded to the terminal as a mouse event.
2951	        if (!had_focus and button == .left) {
2952	            priv.suppress_left_mouse_release = true;
2953	            return;
2954	        }
2955	
2956	        if (button == .middle and
2957	            !priv.gtk_enable_primary_paste and
2958	            !core_surface.mouseReportingActive())
2959	        {
2960	            return;
2961	        }
2962	
2963	        const consumed = consumed: {
2964	            const gtk_mods = event.getModifierState();
2965	            const mods = gtk_key.translateMods(gtk_mods);
2966	            break :consumed core_surface.mouseButtonCallback(
2967	                .press,
2968	                button,
2969	                mods,
2970	            ) catch |err| err: {
2971	                log.warn("error in key callback err={}", .{err});
2972	                break :err false;
2973	            };
2974	        };
2975	
2976	        // If a right click isn't consumed, mouseButtonCallback selects the hovered
2977	        // word and returns false. We can use this to handle the context menu
2978	        // opening under normal scenarios.
2979	        if (!consumed and button == .right) {
3000	    fn gcMouseUp(
3001	        gesture: *gtk.GestureClick,
3002	        _: c_int,
3003	        _: f64,
3004	        _: f64,
3005	        self: *Self,
3006	    ) callconv(.c) void {
3007	        const event = gesture.as(gtk.EventController).getCurrentEvent() orelse return;
3008	
3009	        const priv = self.private();
3010	        const surface = priv.core_surface orelse return;
3011	        const gtk_mods = event.getModifierState();
3012	        const button = translateMouseButton(gesture.as(gtk.GestureSingle).getCurrentButton());
3013	
3014	        if (button == .left and priv.suppress_left_mouse_release) {
3015	            priv.suppress_left_mouse_release = false;
3016	            return;
3017	        }
3018	
3019	        if (button == .middle and
3020	            !priv.gtk_enable_primary_paste and
3021	            !surface.mouseReportingActive())
3022	        {
3023	            return;
3024	        }
3025	
3026	        const mods = gtk_key.translateMods(gtk_mods);
3027	        const consumed = surface.mouseButtonCallback(
3028	            .release,
3029	            button,
3030	            mods,
3031	        ) catch |err| {
3032	            log.warn("error in key callback err={}", .{err});
3033	            return;
3034	        };
3035	
3036	        // Trigger the on-screen keyboard if we have no selection,
3037	        // and that the mouse event hasn't been intercepted by the callback.
3038	        //
3039	        // It's better to do this here rather than within the core callback
3040	        // since we have direct access to the underlying gdk.Event here.
3041	        if (!consumed and button == .left and !surface.hasSelection()) {
3042	            if (!self.showOnScreenKeyboard(event)) {
3043	                log.warn("failed to activate the on-screen keyboard", .{});
3044	            }
3045	        }
3046	    }
3339	    fn imCommit(
3340	        _: *gtk.IMMulticontext,
3341	        bytes: [*:0]u8,
3342	        self: *Self,
3343	    ) callconv(.c) void {
3344	        const priv = self.private();
3345	        const str = std.mem.sliceTo(bytes, 0);
3346	
3347	        // log.debug("GTKIM: input commit composing={} keyevent={} str={s}", .{
3348	        //     self.im_composing,
3349	        //     self.in_keyevent,
3350	        //     str,
3351	        // });
3352	
3353	        // We need to handle commit specially if we're in a key event.
3354	        // Specifically, GTK will send us a commit event for basic key
3355	        // encodings like "a" (on a US layout keyboard). We don't want
3356	        // to treat this as IME committed text because we want to associate
3357	        // it with a key event (i.e. "a" key press).
3358	        switch (priv.in_keyevent) {
3359	            // If we're not in a key event then this commit is from
3360	            // some other source (i.e. on-screen keyboard, tablet, etc.)
3361	            // and we want to commit the text to the core surface.
3362	            .false => {},
3363	
3364	            // If we're in a composing state and in a key event then this
3365	            // key event is resulting in a commit of multiple keypresses
3366	            // and we don't want to encode it alongside the keypress.
3367	            .composing => {},
3368	
3369	            // If we're not composing then this commit is just a normal
3370	            // key encoding and we want our key event to handle it so
3371	            // that Ghostty can be aware of the key event alongside
3372	            // the text.
3373	            .not_composing => {
3374	                if (str.len > priv.im_buf.len) {
3375	                    log.warn("not enough buffer space for input method commit", .{});
3376	                    return;
3377	                }
3378	
3379	                // Copy our committed text to the buffer
3380	                @memcpy(priv.im_buf[0..str.len], str);
3381	                priv.im_len = @intCast(str.len);
3382	
3383	                // log.debug("input commit len={}", .{priv.im_len});
3384	                return;
3385	            },
3386	        }
3387	
3388	        // If we reach this point from above it means we're composing OR
3389	        // not in a keypress. In either case, we want to commit the text
3390	        // given to us because that's what GTK is asking us to do. If we're
3391	        // not in a keypress it means that this commit came via a non-keyboard
3392	        // event (i.e. on-screen keyboard, tablet of some kind, etc.).
3393	
3394	        // Committing ends composing state
3395	        priv.im_composing = false;
3396	
3397	        // We can't set our preedit on our surface unless we're realized.
3398	        // We do this now because we want to still keep our input method
3399	        // state coherent.
3400	        if (priv.core_surface) |surface| {
3401	            // End our preedit state. Well-behaved input methods do this for us
3402	            // by triggering a preedit-end event but some do not (ibus 1.5.29).
3403	            surface.preeditCallback(null) catch |err| {
3404	                log.warn("error in preedit callback err={}", .{err});
3405	            };
3406	
3407	            // Send the text to the core surface, associated with no key (an
3408	            // invalid key, which should produce no PTY encoding).
3409	            _ = surface.keyCallback(.{
3410	                .action = .press,
3411	                .key = .unidentified,
3412	                .mods = .{},
3413	                .consumed_mods = .{},
3414	                .composing = false,
3415	                .utf8 = str,
3416	            }) catch |err| {
3417	                log.warn("error in key callback err={}", .{err});
3418	            };
3419	        }
3420	    }
3497	    fn renderSurfaceResize(
3498	        _: *RenderSurface,
3499	        width: c_int,
3500	        height: c_int,
3501	        self: *Self,
3502	    ) callconv(.c) void {
3503	        // Some debug output to help understand what GTK is telling us.
3504	        {
3505	            const widget = self.private().render_surface.as(gtk.Widget);
3506	            log.debug("gl resize width={} height={} scale={d} scale_factor={}", .{
3507	                width,
3508	                height,
3509	                scale_util.widgetSurfaceScale(widget),
3510	                widget.getScaleFactor(),
3511	            });
3512	        }
3513	
3514	        // Store our cached size
3515	        const priv = self.private();
3516	
3517	        const new_size: apprt.SurfaceSize = .{
3518	            .width = @intCast(width),
3519	            .height = @intCast(height),
3520	        };
3521	        const changed = !priv.size.eql(&new_size);
3522	        priv.size = new_size;
3523	
3524	        // If our surface is initialized, we send callbacks.
3525	        if (priv.core_surface) |surface| {
3526	            // We also update the content scale because there is no signal for
3527	            // content scale change and it seems to trigger a resize event.
3528	            surface.contentScaleCallback(self.getContentScale()) catch |err| {
3529	                log.warn("error in content scale callback err={}", .{err});
3530	            };
3531	
3532	            if (changed) {
3533	                surface.sizeCallback(new_size) catch |err| {
3534	                    log.warn("error in size callback err={}", .{err});
3535	                };
3536	                // Setup our resize overlay if configured
3537	                self.resizeOverlaySchedule();
3538	            }
3539	        } else {
3540	            // If we haven't initalized a surface yet, now's the time to
3541	            // do so. We cannot do this any earlier since GTK defers the
3542	            // layouting and size allocation until after widget initialization.
3543	            // Note that initialization is different from realization:
3544	            // `initSurface` will start the core surface in an unrealized
3545	            // state and wait for the `realize` signal, unless the widget
3546	            // is somehow realized before the first resize signal ever fires.
3547	            self.initSurface() catch |err| {
3548	                log.warn("surface failed to initialize err={}", .{err});
3549	            };
3550	        }
3551	    }
3557	    fn initSurface(self: *Self) InitError!void {
3558	        const priv: *Private = self.private();
3559	        assert(priv.core_surface == null);
3560	
3561	        const app = Application.default();
3562	        const alloc = app.allocator();
3563	
3564	        // Make our pointer to store our surface
3565	        const surface = try alloc.create(CoreSurface);
3566	        errdefer alloc.destroy(surface);
3567	
3568	        // Add ourselves to the list of surfaces on the app.
3569	        try app.core().addSurface(self.rt());
3570	        errdefer app.core().deleteSurface(self.rt());
3610	        // Initialize the surface
3611	        surface.init(
3612	            alloc,
3613	            &config,
3614	            app.core(),
3615	            app.rt(),
3616	            &priv.rt_surface,
3617	        ) catch |err| {
3618	            log.warn("failed to initialize surface err={}", .{err});
3619	            return error.SurfaceError;
3620	        };
3621	        errdefer surface.deinit();
3622	
3623	        // Store it!
3624	        priv.core_surface = surface;
3625	        priv.desktop_notifications = .init(surface.id);

39	/// The implementation to use for the app runtime. This is comptime chosen
40	/// so that every build has exactly one application runtime implementation.
41	/// Note: it is very rare to use Runtime directly; most usage will use
42	/// Window or something.
43	pub const runtime = switch (build_config.artifact) {
44	    .exe => switch (build_config.app_runtime) {
45	        .none => none,
46	        .gtk => gtk,
47	    },
48	    .lib => embedded,
49	    .wasm_module => browser,
50	};
51	
52	pub const App = runtime.App;

==== src/termio/Thread.zig ====
127	pub fn deinit(self: *Thread) void {
128	    self.scroll.deinit();
129	    self.coalesce.deinit();
130	    self.sync_reset.deinit();
131	    self.stop.deinit();
132	    self.loop.deinit();
133	}
259	    // This is the data sent to xev callbacks. We want a pointer to both
260	    // ourselves and the thread data so we can thread that through (pun intended).
261	    var cb: CallbackData = .{ .self = self, .io = io };
262	
263	    // Run our thread start/end callbacks. This allows the implementation
264	    // to hook into the event loop as needed. The thread data is created
265	    // on the stack here so that it has a stable pointer throughout the
266	    // lifetime of the thread.
267	    try io.threadEnter(self, &cb.data);
268	    defer cb.data.deinit();
269	    defer io.threadExit(&cb.data);
270	
271	    // Start the async handlers.
272	    mailbox.wakeup.wait(&self.loop, &self.wakeup_c, CallbackData, &cb, wakeupCallback);
273	    self.stop.wait(&self.loop, &self.stop_c, CallbackData, &cb, stopCallback);
274	
275	    // Run
276	    log.debug("starting IO thread", .{});
277	    defer log.debug("starting IO thread shutdown", .{});
278	    try self.loop.run(.until_done);
279	}
280	
281	/// This is the data passed to xev callbacks on the thread.
282	const CallbackData = struct {
283	    self: *Thread,
284	    io: *termio.Termio,
285	    data: termio.Termio.ThreadData = undefined,
286	};
321	            .change_config => |config| {
322	                defer config.alloc.destroy(config.ptr);
323	                try io.changeConfig(data, config.ptr);
324	            },
326	            .resize => |v| self.handleResize(cb, v),
349	            .write_small => |v| try io.queueWrite(
350	                data,
351	                v.data[0..v.len],
352	                self.flags.linefeed_mode,
353	            ),
354	            .write_stable => |v| try io.queueWrite(
355	                data,
356	                v,
357	                self.flags.linefeed_mode,
358	            ),
359	            .write_alloc => |v| {
360	                defer v.alloc.free(v.data);
361	                try io.queueWrite(
362	                    data,
363	                    v.data,
364	                    self.flags.linefeed_mode,
365	                );
366	            },
389	fn handleResize(self: *Thread, cb: *CallbackData, resize: renderer.Size) void {
390	    self.coalesce_data.resize = resize;
391	
392	    // If the timer is already active we just return. In the future we want
393	    // to reset the timer up to a maximum wait time but for now this ensures
394	    // relatively smooth resizing.
395	    if (self.coalesce_c.state() == .active) return;
396	
397	    self.coalesce.reset(
398	        &self.loop,
399	        &self.coalesce_c,
400	        &self.coalesce_cancel_c,
401	        Coalesce.min_ms,
402	        CallbackData,
403	        cb,
404	        coalesceCallback,
405	    );
406	}
427	fn coalesceCallback(
428	    cb_: ?*CallbackData,
429	    _: *xev.Loop,
430	    _: *xev.Completion,
431	    r: xev.Timer.RunError!void,
432	) xev.CallbackAction {
433	    _ = r catch |err| switch (err) {
434	        error.Canceled => {},
435	        else => {
436	            log.warn("error during coalesce callback err={}", .{err});
437	            return .disarm;
438	        },
439	    };
440	
441	    const cb = cb_ orelse return .disarm;
442	
443	    if (cb.self.coalesce_data.resize) |v| {
444	        cb.self.coalesce_data.resize = null;
445	        cb.io.resize(&cb.data, v) catch |err| {
446	            log.warn("error during resize err={}", .{err});
447	        };
448	    }
449	
450	    return .disarm;
451	}
452	
453	fn wakeupCallback(
454	    cb_: ?*CallbackData,
455	    _: *xev.Loop,
456	    _: *xev.Completion,
457	    r: xev.Async.WaitError!void,
458	) xev.CallbackAction {
459	    _ = r catch |err| {
460	        log.err("error in wakeup err={}", .{err});
461	        return .rearm;
462	    };
463	
464	    // When we wake up, we check the mailbox. Mailbox producers should
465	    // wake up our thread after publishing.
466	    const cb = cb_ orelse return .rearm;
467	    cb.self.drainMailbox(cb) catch |err|
468	        log.err("error draining mailbox err={}", .{err});
469	
470	    return .rearm;
471	}
473	fn stopCallback(
474	    cb_: ?*CallbackData,
475	    _: *xev.Loop,
476	    _: *xev.Completion,
477	    r: xev.Async.WaitError!void,
478	) xev.CallbackAction {
479	    _ = r catch unreachable;
480	    cb_.?.self.loop.stop();
481	    return .disarm;
482	}

172	pub fn updateConfig(self: *App, rt_app: *apprt.App, config: *const Config) !void {

175	        try surface.core().handleMessage(.{ .change_config = config });
176	    }
293	            .surface_message => |msg| try self.surfaceMessage(msg.surface, msg.message),
319	pub fn newWindow(self: *App, rt_app: *apprt.App, msg: Message.NewWindow) !void {
320	    const target: apprt.Target = target: {
321	        const parent = msg.parent orelse break :target .app;
322	        if (self.hasSurface(parent)) break :target .{ .surface = parent };
323	        break :target .app;
324	    };
325	
326	    _ = try rt_app.performAction(
327	        target,
328	        .new_window,
329	        {},
330	    );
331	}
444	pub fn performAction(
445	    self: *App,
446	    rt_app: *apprt.App,
447	    action: input.Binding.Action.Scoped(.app),
448	) !void {
449	    switch (action) {
450	        .unbind => unreachable,
451	        .ignore => {},
452	        .quit => _ = try rt_app.performAction(.app, .quit, {}),
453	        .new_window => _ = try self.newWindow(rt_app, .{ .parent = null }),
454	        .open_config => |v| _ = try rt_app.performAction(
455	            .app,
456	            .open_config,
457	            switch (v) {
458	                .os_open => .os_open,
459	                .new_window => .new_window,
460	            },
461	        ),
462	        .reload_config => _ = try rt_app.performAction(.app, .reload_config, .{}),
463	        .set_window_theme => |v| _ = try rt_app.performAction(
464	            .app,
465	            .set_window_theme,
466	            switch (v) {
467	                .config => .config,
468	                .system => .system,
469	                .light => .light,
470	                .dark => .dark,
471	            },
472	        ),
473	        .close_all_windows => _ = try rt_app.performAction(.app, .close_all_windows, {}),
474	        .toggle_quick_terminal => _ = try rt_app.performAction(.app, .toggle_quick_terminal, {}),
475	        .toggle_visibility => _ = try rt_app.performAction(.app, .toggle_visibility, {}),
476	        .check_for_updates => _ = try rt_app.performAction(.app, .check_for_updates, {}),
477	        .show_gtk_inspector => _ = try rt_app.performAction(.app, .show_gtk_inspector, {}),
478	        .undo => _ = try rt_app.performAction(.app, .undo, {}),
479	
480	        .redo => _ = try rt_app.performAction(.app, .redo, {}),
481	    }
482	}
484	/// Performs a chained action. We will continue executing each action
485	/// even if there is a failure in a prior action.
486	pub fn performAllChainedAction(
487	    self: *App,
488	    rt_app: *apprt.App,
489	    actions: []const input.Binding.Action,
490	) void {
491	    for (actions) |action| {
492	        self.performAllAction(rt_app, action) catch |err| {
493	            log.warn("error performing chained action action={s} err={}", .{
494	                @tagName(action),
495	                err,
496	            });
497	        };
498	    }
499	}
500	
501	/// Perform an app-wide binding action. If the action is surface-specific
502	/// then it will be performed on all surfaces. To perform only app-scoped
503	/// actions, use performAction.
504	pub fn performAllAction(
505	    self: *App,
506	    rt_app: *apprt.App,
507	    action: input.Binding.Action,
508	) !void {
509	    switch (action.scope()) {
510	        // App-scoped actions are handled by the app so that they aren't
511	        // repeated for each surface (since each surface forwards
512	        // app-scoped actions back up).
513	        .app => try self.performAction(
514	            rt_app,
515	            action.scoped(.app).?, // asserted through the scope match
516	        ),
517	
518	        // Surface-scoped actions are performed on all surfaces. Errors
519	        // are logged but processing continues.
520	        .surface => for (self.surfaces.items) |surface| {
521	            _ = surface.core().performBindingAction(action) catch |err| {
522	                log.warn("error performing binding action on surface id={x} err={}", .{
523	                    surface.core().id,
524	                    err,
525	                });
526	            };
527	        },
528	    }
529	}
532	fn surfaceMessage(self: *App, surface: *Surface, msg: apprt.surface.Message) !void {
533	    // We want to ensure our window is still active. Window messages
534	    // are quite rare and we normally don't have many windows so we do
535	    // a simple linear search here.
536	    if (self.hasSurface(surface)) {
537	        try surface.handleMessage(msg);
538	    }
539	
540	    // Window was not found, it probably quit before we handled the message.
541	    // Not a problem.
542	}
543	
544	fn hasSurface(self: *const App, surface: *const Surface) bool {
545	    for (self.surfaces.items) |v| {
546	        if (v.core() == surface) return true;
547	    }
548	
549	    return false;
550	}
597	pub const Mailbox = struct {
598	    /// The type used for sending messages to the app thread.
599	    pub const Queue = BlockingQueue(Message, 64);
600	
601	    rt_app: *apprt.App,
602	    mailbox: *Queue,
603	
604	    /// Send a message to the surface.
605	    pub fn push(self: Mailbox, msg: Message, timeout: Queue.Timeout) Queue.Size {
606	        const result = self.mailbox.push(global.io(), msg, timeout);
607	
608	        // Wake up our app loop
609	        self.rt_app.wakeup();
610	
611	        return result;
612	    }
613	};

86	    /// Initialize a change_config message.
87	    pub fn initChangeConfig(alloc: Allocator, config: *const configpkg.Config) !Message {
88	        const thread_ptr = try alloc.create(renderer.Thread.DerivedConfig);
89	        errdefer alloc.destroy(thread_ptr);
90	        const config_ptr = try alloc.create(renderer.Renderer.DerivedConfig);
91	        errdefer alloc.destroy(config_ptr);
92	
93	        thread_ptr.* = renderer.Thread.DerivedConfig.init(config);
94	        config_ptr.* = try renderer.Renderer.DerivedConfig.init(alloc, config);
95	        errdefer config_ptr.deinit();
96	
97	        return .{
98	            .change_config = .{
99	                .alloc = alloc,
100	                .thread = thread_ptr,
101	                .impl = config_ptr,
102	            },
103	        };

==== src/renderer/Thread.zig ====
177	pub fn deinit(self: *Thread) void {
178	    self.stop.deinit();
179	    self.wakeup.deinit();
180	    self.render_h.deinit();
181	    self.draw_now.deinit();
182	    self.cursor_h.deinit();
183	    if (comptime terminalpkg.compression_enabled)
184	        self.compression.deinit();
185	    self.loop.deinit();
186	
187	    // Nothing can possibly access the mailbox anymore, destroy it.
188	    self.mailbox.destroy(self.alloc);
189	}
220	    // Run our loop start/end callbacks if the renderer cares.
221	    const has_loop = @hasDecl(rendererpkg.Renderer, "loopEnter");
222	    if (has_loop) try self.renderer.loopEnter(self);
223	    defer if (has_loop) self.renderer.loopExit();
224	
225	    // Run our thread start/end callbacks. This is important because some
226	    // renderers have to do per-thread setup. For example, OpenGL has to set
227	    // some thread-local state since that is how it works.
228	    try self.renderer.threadEnter(self.surface);
229	    defer self.renderer.threadExit();
230	
231	    // Start the async handlers
232	    self.wakeup.wait(&self.loop, &self.wakeup_c, Thread, self, wakeupCallback);
233	    self.stop.wait(&self.loop, &self.stop_c, Thread, self, stopCallback);
234	    self.draw_now.wait(&self.loop, &self.draw_now_c, Thread, self, drawNowCallback);
397	            .font_grid => |grid| {
398	                self.renderer.setFontGrid(grid.grid);
399	                grid.set.deref(grid.old_key);
400	            },
404	            .resize => |v| self.renderer.setScreenSize(v),
406	            .change_config => |config| {
407	                defer config.alloc.destroy(config.thread);
408	                defer config.alloc.destroy(config.impl);
409	                try self.changeConfig(config.thread);
410	                try self.renderer.changeConfig(config.impl);
411	
412	                // The config affects what animation wakes the
413	                // renderer needs (custom shaders, animation mode).
414	                self.armAnimationTimer();
415	            },
417	            .search_viewport_matches => |v| {
418	                // Note we don't free the new value because we expect our
419	                // allocators to match.
420	                if (self.renderer.search_matches) |*m| m.arena.deinit();
421	                self.renderer.search_matches = v;
422	                self.renderer.search_matches_dirty = true;
423	            },
424	
425	            .search_selected_match => |v| {
426	                // Note we don't free the new value because we expect our
427	                // allocators to match.
428	                if (self.renderer.search_selected_match) |*m| m.arena.deinit();
429	                self.renderer.search_selected_match = v;
430	                self.renderer.search_matches_dirty = true;
431	            },

446	fn changeConfig(self: *Thread, config: *const DerivedConfig) !void {
447	    // A newly enabled scheduler must reconsider existing history even when no
448	    // terminal activity occurred while compression was disabled.
449	    if (comptime terminalpkg.compression_enabled) {
450	        if (!self.config.scrollback_compression and
451	            config.scrollback_compression)
452	        {
453	            self.compression.activity = null;
454	        }
455	    }
456	
457	    self.config = config.*;
458	}
474	fn wakeupCallback(
475	    self_: ?*Thread,
476	    _: *xev.Loop,
477	    _: *xev.Completion,
478	    r: xev.Async.WaitError!void,
479	) xev.CallbackAction {
480	    _ = r catch |err| {
481	        log.err("error in wakeup err={}", .{err});
482	        return .rearm;
483	    };
484	
485	    const t = self_.?;
486	
487	    // When we wake up, we check the mailbox. Mailbox producers should
488	    // wake up our thread after publishing.
489	    t.drainMailbox() catch |err|
490	        log.err("error draining mailbox err={}", .{err});
491	
492	    // Render immediately
493	    _ = renderCallback(t, undefined, undefined, {});
722	fn stopCallback(
723	    self_: ?*Thread,
724	    _: *xev.Loop,
725	    _: *xev.Completion,
726	    r: xev.Async.WaitError!void,
727	) xev.CallbackAction {
728	    _ = r catch unreachable;
729	    self_.?.loop.stop();
730	    return .disarm;
731	}

41	/// The display link used to pace drawing to the display's refresh

627	            pub fn init(
628	                alloc_gpa: Allocator,
629	                config: *const configpkg.Config,
630	            ) !DerivedConfig {
631	                var arena = ArenaAllocator.init(alloc_gpa);
632	                errdefer arena.deinit();
633	                const alloc = arena.allocator();
634	
635	                // Copy our shaders
636	                const custom_shaders = try config.@"custom-shader".clone(alloc);
637	
638	                // Copy our background image
639	                const bg_image =
640	                    if (config.@"background-image") |bg|
641	                        try bg.clone(alloc)
642	                    else
643	                        null;
644	
645	                // Copy our font features
646	                const font_features = try config.@"font-feature".clone(alloc);
647	
648	                // Get our font styles
649	                var font_styles = font.CodepointResolver.StyleStatus.initFill(true);
650	                font_styles.set(.bold, config.@"font-style-bold" != .false);
651	                font_styles.set(.italic, config.@"font-style-italic" != .false);
652	                font_styles.set(.bold_italic, config.@"font-style-bold-italic" != .false);

656	                    alloc,
657	                    config.link.links.items,
658	                );
659	
660	                return .{
661	                    .background_opacity = @max(0, @min(1, config.@"background-opacity")),
662	                    .background_opacity_cells = config.@"background-opacity-cells",
663	                    .font_thicken = config.@"font-thicken",
664	                    .font_thicken_strength = config.@"font-thicken-strength",

676	                    .faint_opacity = @intFromFloat(@ceil(config.@"faint-opacity" * 255)),

841	        pub fn deinit(self: *Self) void {
842	            // This only deinitializes and frees CPU-side state
843	            // and does not free GPU resources like the swap chain and
844	            // shaders. Those are freed with `releaseGpuResources`.
845	
846	            if (self.overlay) |*overlay| overlay.deinit(self.alloc);
847	            self.terminal_state.deinit(self.alloc);
848	            if (self.search_selected_match) |*m| m.arena.deinit();
849	            if (self.search_matches) |*m| m.arena.deinit();
850	
851	            self.latest_frame.deinit(global.io());
852	
853	            // Stop and release the display link. Surfaces are destroyed
854	            // on the main thread, where `close` stops the link before
855	            // returning. After this the display link callback is never
856	            // invoked again, so `renderer.Thread` can free the `draw_now`
857	            // handle the callback points at.
858	            if (DisplayLink != void) {
859	                if (self.display_link) |display_link| {
860	                    display_link.close();
861	                    self.display_link = null;
862	                }
863	            }
864	
865	            self.cells.deinit(self.alloc);
866	
867	            self.font_shaper.deinit();
868	            self.font_shaper_cache.deinit(self.alloc);
869	
870	            self.config.deinit();
871	            self.api.deinit();
872	
873	            self.* = undefined;
874	        }
911	        /// Callback called by renderer.Thread when it exits. Called on the
912	        /// render thread. Releases all GPU resources before the API tears down
913	        /// its context, since after this the GL context will be gone.
914	        pub fn threadExit(self: *Self) void {
915	            {
916	                self.draw_mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
917	                defer self.draw_mutex.unlock(global.io());
918	
919	                // Release swap chain and shaders. Shaders are only freed
920	                // while unrealized and not every apprt unrealizes before
921	                // destroying a surface (macOS never does), so mark it
922	                // unrealized. This also stops any later draw.
923	                self.display_realized = false;
924	                self.releaseGpuResources();
925	
926	                // We don't release images in `releaseGpuResources`
927	                // since it can be called whenever the terminal is
928	                // occluded or unrealized, and we don't want to
929	                // reupload images every time that happens.
930	                self.images.deinit(self.alloc);
931	                self.images = .empty;
932	
933	                if (self.bg_image) |img| {
934	                    img.deinit(self.alloc);
935	                    self.bg_image = null;
936	                }
937	            }
938	
939	            // If our API has to do things on thread exit, let it.
940	            if (@hasDecl(GraphicsAPI, "threadExit")) {
941	                self.api.threadExit();
942	            }
943	        }
944	
945	        /// Called by renderer.Thread when it starts the main loop.
946	        pub fn loopEnter(self: *Self, thr: *renderer.Thread) !void {
947	            // If our API has to do things on loop enter, let it.
948	            if (@hasDecl(GraphicsAPI, "loopEnter")) {
949	                self.api.loopEnter();
950	            }
951	
952	            // If we don't support a display link we have no work to do.
953	            if (comptime DisplayLink == void) return;
954	
955	            self.syncDisplayLink(null, &thr.draw_now);
956	        }
957	
958	        /// Called by renderer.Thread when it exits the main loop.
959	        pub fn loopExit(self: *Self) void {
960	            // If our API has to do things on loop exit, let it.
961	            if (@hasDecl(GraphicsAPI, "loopExit")) {
962	                self.api.loopExit();
963	            }
964	
965	            // If we don't support a display link we have no work to do.
966	            if (comptime DisplayLink == void) return;
967	
968	            // Ask the display link to stop. The stop itself happens on
969	            // the main thread shortly after. `deinit` later stops the
970	            // link for certain before anything the display link callback
971	            // references is freed.
972	            const display_link = self.display_link orelse return;
973	            display_link.setRunning(false);
974	        }
1242	        /// Caller must lock the draw mutex before calling this function.
1243	        /// Resources that are already released are skipped.
1244	        pub fn releaseGpuResources(self: *Self) void {
1245	            if (self.swap_chain) |*sc| {
1246	                // Waits for any in-flight frames to complete, then
1247	                // frees all GPU resources.
1248	                sc.deinit();
1249	                self.swap_chain = null;
1250	            }
1251	
1252	            // Release the shaders as well if we're unrealized.
1253	            if (!self.display_realized) {
1254	                self.shaders.deinit(self.alloc);
1255	            }
1256	        }
1273	        fn syncDisplayLink(
1274	            self: *Self,
1275	            display_id: ?u32,
1276	            draw_now: ?*xev.Async,
1277	        ) void {
1278	            if (comptime DisplayLink == void) return;
1279	
1280	            const display_link = self.display_link orelse display_link: {
1281	                if (!self.config.vsync) return;
1282	                const callback = draw_now orelse return;
1283	                const result = DisplayLink.create(
1284	                    self.alloc,
1285	                    xev.Async,
1286	                    &displayLinkCallback,
1287	                    callback,
1288	                ) catch |err| {
1289	                    // A locked macOS session can temporarily have no active
1290	                    // displays. Rendering continues without vsync and the
1291	                    // next call to this function tries again.
1292	                    log.warn("error creating display link; using fallback rendering err={}", .{err});
1293	                    return;
1294	                };
1295	
1296	                self.display_link = result;
1297	                log.info("created display link", .{});
1298	                break :display_link result;
1299	            };
1300	
1301	            if (display_id) |id| display_link.setDisplay(id);
1302	
1303	            const should_run =
1304	                // Non-visible windows never vsync
1305	                self.visible and
1306	                // Only vsync if we have cell or image changes or animation
1307	                (self.cells_rebuilt or
1308	                    self.images_rebuilt or
1309	                    self.animationWake() != null);
1310	
1311	            display_link.setRunning(should_run);
1312	        }
1547	                const overlay_features: []const Overlay.Feature = overlay: {
1548	                    const insp = state.inspector orelse break :overlay &.{};
1549	                    const renderer_info = insp.rendererInfo();
1550	                    break :overlay renderer_info.overlayFeatures(
1551	                        arena_alloc,
1552	                    ) catch &.{};
1553	                };
1554	
1555	                break :critical .{
1556	                    .links = links,
1557	                    .mouse = state.mouse,
1558	                    .preedit = preedit,
1559	                    .scrollbar = scrollbar,
1560	                    .overlay_features = overlay_features,
2171	        pub fn changeConfig(self: *Self, config: *DerivedConfig) !void {
2172	            self.draw_mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
2173	            defer self.draw_mutex.unlock(global.io());
2174	
2175	            // We always redo the font shaper in case font features changed. We
2176	            // could check to see if there was an actual config change but this is
2177	            // easier and rare enough to not cause performance issues.
2178	            {
2179	                var font_shaper = try font.Shaper.init(self.alloc, .{
2180	                    .features = config.font_features.items,
2181	                });
2182	                errdefer font_shaper.deinit();
2183	                self.font_shaper.deinit();
2184	                self.font_shaper = font_shaper;
2185	            }
2186	
2187	            // We also need to reset the shaper cache so shaper info
2188	            // from the previous font isn't reused for the new font.
2189	            const font_shaper_cache = font.ShaperCache.init();
2190	            self.font_shaper_cache.deinit(self.alloc);
2191	            self.font_shaper_cache = font_shaper_cache;
2192	
2193	            // Set our new minimum contrast
2194	            self.uniforms.min_contrast = config.min_contrast;
2195	
2196	            // Set our new color space and blending
2197	            self.uniforms.bools.use_display_p3 = config.colorspace == .@"display-p3";
2198	            self.uniforms.bools.use_linear_blending = config.blending.isLinear();
2199	            self.uniforms.bools.use_linear_correction = config.blending == .@"linear-corrected";
2200	
2201	            const bg_image_config_changed =
2202	                self.config.bg_image_fit != config.bg_image_fit or
2203	                self.config.bg_image_position != config.bg_image_position or
2204	                self.config.bg_image_repeat != config.bg_image_repeat or
2205	                self.config.bg_image_opacity != config.bg_image_opacity;
2206	
2207	            const bg_image_changed =
2208	                if (self.config.bg_image) |old|
2209	                    if (config.bg_image) |new|
2210	                        !old.equal(new)
2211	                    else
2212	                        true
2213	                else
2214	                    config.bg_image != null;
2215	
2216	            const old_blending = self.config.blending;
2217	            const custom_shaders_changed = !self.config.custom_shaders.equal(config.custom_shaders);
2218	
2219	            self.config.deinit();
2220	            self.config = config.*;
2221	
2222	            // If our background image path changed, prepare the new bg image.
2223	            if (bg_image_changed) try self.prepBackgroundImage();
2224	
2225	            // If our background image config changed, update the vertex buffer.
2226	            if (bg_image_config_changed) self.updateBgImageBuffer();
2227	
2228	            // Reset our viewport to force a rebuild, in case of a font change.
2229	            self.markDirty();
2230	
2231	            const blending_changed = old_blending != config.blending;
2232	
2233	            if (blending_changed) {
2234	                // We update our API's blending mode.
2235	                self.api.blending = config.blending;
2236	                // And indicate that we need to reinitialize our shaders.
2237	                self.reinitialize_shaders = true;
2238	                // And indicate that our swap chain targets need to
2239	                // be re-created to account for the new blending mode.
2240	                self.target_config_modified +%= 1;
2241	            }
2242	
2243	            if (custom_shaders_changed) {
2244	                self.reinitialize_shaders = true;
2245	            }
2246	        }
2248	        /// Resize the screen.
2249	        pub fn setScreenSize(
2250	            self: *Self,
2251	            size: renderer.Size,
2252	        ) void {
2253	            self.draw_mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
2254	            defer self.draw_mutex.unlock(global.io());
2255	
2256	            self.size = size;
2257	            self.updateScreenSizeUniforms();
2258	
2259	            // Some graphics APIs need to manually update their viewport,
2260	            // like OpenGL. Do so here.
2261	            if (@hasDecl(GraphicsAPI, "setViewport")) {
2262	                self.api.setViewport(self.size.screen.width, self.size.screen.height);
2263	            }
2264	
2265	            log.debug("screen size size={}", .{size});
2266	        }
2267	
2268	        /// Update uniforms that are based on the screen size.
2269	        ///
2270	        /// Caller must hold the draw mutex.
2271	        fn updateScreenSizeUniforms(self: *Self) void {
2272	            const terminal_size = self.size.terminal();
2273	
2274	            // Blank space around the grid.
2275	            const blank: renderer.Padding = self.size.screen.blankPadding(
2276	                self.size.padding,
2277	                .{
2278	                    .columns = self.cells.size.columns,
2279	                    .rows = self.cells.size.rows,
2280	                },
2281	                .{
2282	                    .width = self.grid_metrics.cell_width,
2283	                    .height = self.grid_metrics.cell_height,
2284	                },
2285	            ).add(self.size.padding);
2286	
2287	            // Setup our uniforms
2288	            self.uniforms.projection_matrix = @bitCast(math.ortho2d(
2289	                -1 * @as(f32, @floatFromInt(self.size.padding.left)),
2290	                @floatFromInt(terminal_size.width + self.size.padding.right),
2291	                @floatFromInt(terminal_size.height + self.size.padding.bottom),
2292	                -1 * @as(f32, @floatFromInt(self.size.padding.top)),
2293	            ));
2294	            self.uniforms.grid_padding = .{
2295	                @floatFromInt(blank.top),
2296	                @floatFromInt(blank.right),
2297	                @floatFromInt(blank.bottom),
2298	                @floatFromInt(blank.left),
2299	            };
2300	            self.uniforms.screen_size = .{
2301	                @floatFromInt(self.size.screen.width),
2302	                @floatFromInt(self.size.screen.height),
2303	            };
2304	        }
==== src/renderer/size.zig ====
8	pub const PaddingBalance = enum {
9	    /// No balancing; padding is applied as specified explicitly.
10	    false,
11	    /// Balances padding but caps the top padding so the first row doesn't
12	    /// drift too far from the top of the window. Excess vertical space is
13	    /// shifted to the bottom.
14	    true,
15	    /// Distributes leftover space equally on all sides so the grid is
16	    /// centered within the screen.
17	    equal,
18	};
19	
20	/// All relevant sizes for a rendered terminal. These are all the sizes that
21	/// any functionality should need to know about the terminal in order to
22	/// convert between any coordinate systems.
23	///
24	/// See the individual field type documentation for more information on each
25	/// field. One important note is that any pixel values should already be scaled
26	/// to the current DPI of the screen. If the DPI changes, the sizes should be
27	/// recalculated and we expect this to be done by the caller.
28	pub const Size = struct {
29	    screen: ScreenSize,
30	    cell: CellSize,
31	    padding: Padding,
32	
33	    /// Return the grid size for this size. The grid size is calculated by
34	    /// taking the screen size, removing padding, and dividing by the cell
35	    /// dimensions.
36	    pub fn grid(self: Size) GridSize {
37	        return .init(self.screen.subPadding(self.padding), self.cell);
38	    }
39	
40	    /// The size of the terminal. This is the same as the screen without
41	    /// padding.
42	    pub fn terminal(self: Size) ScreenSize {
43	        return self.screen.subPadding(self.padding);
44	    }
45	
46	    /// Set the padding to be balanced around the grid. The balanced
47	    /// padding is calculated AFTER the explicit padding is taken
48	    /// into account.
49	    pub fn balancePadding(
50	        self: *Size,
51	        explicit: Padding,
52	        mode: PaddingBalance,
53	    ) void {
54	        // This ensure grid() does the right thing
55	        self.padding = explicit;
56	
57	        // Now we can calculate the balanced padding
58	        self.padding = .balanced(
59	            self.screen,
60	            self.grid(),
61	            self.cell,
62	        );
63	
64	        switch (mode) {
65	            .false => unreachable,
66	            .equal => {},
67	            .true => {
68	                // Cap the top padding to avoid excessive space above the
69	                // first row. The maximum is the balanced explicit horizontal
70	                // padding plus half a cell width. Any excess is shifted to
71	                // the bottom.
72	                const max_top = (explicit.left + explicit.right + self.cell.width) / 2;
73	                const vshift = self.padding.top -| max_top;
74	                self.padding.top -= vshift;
75	                self.padding.bottom += vshift;
76	            },
77	        }
78	    }
79	};
186	pub const CellSize = extern struct {
187	    width: u32,
188	    height: u32,
189	};
190	
191	/// The dimensions of the screen that the grid is rendered to. This is the
192	/// terminal screen, so it is likely a subset of the window size. The dimensions
193	/// should be in pixels.
194	pub const ScreenSize = extern struct {
195	    width: u32,
196	    height: u32,
197	
198	    /// Subtract padding from the screen size.
199	    pub fn subPadding(self: ScreenSize, padding: Padding) ScreenSize {
200	        return .{
201	            .width = self.width -| (padding.left + padding.right),
202	            .height = self.height -| (padding.top + padding.bottom),
203	        };
204	    }
231	    /// Returns true if two sizes are equal.
232	    pub fn equals(self: ScreenSize, other: ScreenSize) bool {
233	        return self.width == other.width and self.height == other.height;
234	    }
235	};
236	
237	/// The dimensions of the grid itself, in rows/columns units.
238	pub const GridSize = extern struct {
239	    pub const Unit = terminal_size.CellCountInt;
240	
241	    columns: Unit = 0,
242	    rows: Unit = 0,
243	
244	    /// Initialize a grid size based on a screen and cell size.
245	    pub fn init(screen: ScreenSize, cell: CellSize) GridSize {
246	        var result: GridSize = undefined;
247	        result.update(screen, cell);
248	        return result;
249	    }
250	
251	    /// Update the columns/rows for the grid based on the given screen and
252	    /// cell size.
253	    pub fn update(self: *GridSize, screen: ScreenSize, cell: CellSize) void {
254	        const cell_width: f32 = @floatFromInt(cell.width);
255	        const cell_height: f32 = @floatFromInt(cell.height);
256	        const screen_width: f32 = @floatFromInt(screen.width);
257	        const screen_height: f32 = @floatFromInt(screen.height);
258	        const calc_cols: Unit = @intFromFloat(screen_width / cell_width);
259	        const calc_rows: Unit = @intFromFloat(screen_height / cell_height);
260	        self.columns = @max(1, calc_cols);
261	        self.rows = @max(1, calc_rows);
262	    }
270	/// The padding to add to a screen.
271	pub const Padding = extern struct {
272	    top: u32 = 0,
273	    bottom: u32 = 0,
274	    right: u32 = 0,
275	    left: u32 = 0,
276	
277	    /// Returns padding that balances the whitespace around the screen
278	    /// for the given grid and cell sizes.
279	    pub fn balanced(screen: ScreenSize, grid: GridSize, cell: CellSize) Padding {
280	        // Turn our cell sizes into floats for the math
281	        const cell_width: f32 = @floatFromInt(cell.width);
282	        const cell_height: f32 = @floatFromInt(cell.height);
283	
284	        // The size of our full grid
285	        const grid_width = @as(f32, @floatFromInt(grid.columns)) * cell_width;
286	        const grid_height = @as(f32, @floatFromInt(grid.rows)) * cell_height;
287	
288	        // The empty space to the right of a line and bottom of the last row
289	        const space_right = @as(f32, @floatFromInt(screen.width)) - grid_width;
290	        const space_bot = @as(f32, @floatFromInt(screen.height)) - grid_height;
291	
292	        // The padding is split equally along both axes.
293	        const padding_right = @floor(space_right / 2);
294	        const padding_left = padding_right;
295	
296	        const padding_bot = @floor(space_bot / 2);
297	        const padding_top = padding_bot;
298	
299	        const zero = @as(f32, 0);
300	        return .{
301	            .top = @intFromFloat(@max(zero, padding_top)),
302	            .bottom = @intFromFloat(@max(zero, padding_bot)),
303	            .right = @intFromFloat(@max(zero, padding_right)),
304	            .left = @intFromFloat(@max(zero, padding_left)),
305	        };
306	    }
==== src/terminal/Screen.zig ====
3058	/// Set the selection to null to clear any previous selection.
3059	///
3060	/// This is always recommended over setting `selection` directly. Beyond
3061	/// managing memory for you, it also performs safety checks that the selection
3062	/// is always tracked.
3063	pub fn select(self: *Screen, sel_: ?Selection) Allocator.Error!void {
3064	    const sel = sel_ orelse {
3065	        self.clearSelection();
3066	        return;
3067	    };
3068	
3069	    // If this selection is untracked then we track it.
3070	    const tracked_sel = if (sel.tracked()) sel else try sel.track(self);
3071	    errdefer if (!sel.tracked()) tracked_sel.deinit(self);
3072	
3073	    // Untrack prior selection pins that aren't also owned by the replacement.
3074	    // A caller may pass our current tracked selection back to us by value, so
3075	    // releasing both old pins unconditionally would leave the replacement
3076	    // pointing at freed pool entries.
3077	    if (self.selection) |old| {
3078	        const new_bounds = tracked_sel.bounds.tracked;
3079	        switch (old.bounds) {
3080	            .untracked => old.deinit(self),
3081	            .tracked => |old_bounds| {
3082	                if (old_bounds.start != new_bounds.start and
3083	                    old_bounds.start != new_bounds.end)
3084	                {
3085	                    self.pages.untrackPin(old_bounds.start);
3086	                }
3087	                if (old_bounds.end != new_bounds.start and
3088	                    old_bounds.end != new_bounds.end)
3089	                {
3090	                    self.pages.untrackPin(old_bounds.end);
3091	                }
3092	            },
3093	        }
3094	    }
3095	    self.selection = tracked_sel;
3096	    self.dirty.selection = true;
3097	}
3098	
3099	/// Same as select(null) but can't fail.
3100	pub fn clearSelection(self: *Screen) void {
3101	    if (self.selection) |*sel| {
3102	        sel.deinit(self);
3103	        self.dirty.selection = true;
3104	    }
3105	    self.selection = null;
3106	}
