1539	    pub fn scoped(self: Action, comptime s: Scope) ?Scoped(s) {
1540	        switch (self) {
1541	            inline else => |v, tag| {
1542	                // Use comptime to prune out non-app actions
1543	                if (comptime @unionInit(
1544	                    Action,
1545	                    @tagName(tag),
1546	                    undefined,
1547	                ).scope() != s) return null;
1548	
1549	                // Initialize our app action
1550	                return @unionInit(
1551	                    Scoped(s),
1552	                    @tagName(tag),
1553	                    v,
1554	                );
1555	            },
1556	        }
1557	    }
2716	    /// Get an entry for the given key event. This will attempt to find
2717	    /// a binding using multiple parts of the event in the following order:
2718	    ///
2719	    ///   1. Physical key (event.physical_key)
2720	    ///   2. Unshifted Unicode codepoint (event.unshifted_codepoint)
2721	    ///
2722	    pub fn getEvent(self: *const Set, event: KeyEvent) ?Entry {
2723	        var trigger: Trigger = .{
2724	            .mods = event.mods.binding(),
2725	            .key = .{ .physical = event.key },
2726	        };
2727	        if (self.get(trigger)) |v| return v;
2728	
2729	        // If our UTF-8 text is exactly one codepoint, we try to match that.
2730	        if (event.utf8.len > 0) unicode: {
2731	            const view = std.unicode.Utf8View.init(event.utf8) catch break :unicode;
2732	            var it = view.iterator();
2733	
2734	            // No codepoints or multiple codepoints drops to invalid format
2735	            const cp = it.nextCodepoint() orelse break :unicode;
2736	            if (it.nextCodepoint() != null) break :unicode;
2737	
2738	            trigger.key = .{ .unicode = cp };
2739	            if (self.get(trigger)) |v| return v;
2740	        }
2741	
2742	        // Finally fallback to the full unshifted codepoint if we have one.
2743	        // Question: should we be doing this if we have UTF-8 text? I
2744	        // suspect "no" but we don't currently have any failing scenarios
2745	        // to verify this.
2746	        if (event.unshifted_codepoint > 0) {
2747	            trigger.key = .{ .unicode = event.unshifted_codepoint };
2748	            if (self.get(trigger)) |v| return v;
2749	        }
2750	
2751	        // Fallback to catch_all with modifiers first, then without modifiers.
2752	        trigger.key = .catch_all;
2753	        if (self.get(trigger)) |v| return v;
2754	        if (!trigger.mods.empty()) {
2755	            trigger.mods = .{};
2756	            if (self.get(trigger)) |v| return v;
2757	        }
2758	
2759	        return null;
2760	    }
==== src/apprt/gtk/App.zig ====
61	pub fn performAction(
62	    self: *App,
63	    target: apprt.Target,
64	    comptime action: apprt.Action.Key,
65	    value: apprt.Action.Value(action),
66	) !bool {
67	    return try self.app.performAction(target, action, value);
68	}
==== src/input/key_encode.zig ====
14	pub const Options = struct {
15	    /// Terminal DEC mode 1
16	    cursor_key_application: bool = false,
17	
18	    /// Terminal DEC mode 66
19	    keypad_key_application: bool = false,
20	
21	    // DEC Backarrow Key Mode (DECBKM)
22	    // See https://vt100.net/dec/ek-vt3xx-tp-002.pdf page 170
23	    // If `false` (the default), `backspace` emits 0x7f
24	    // If `true`, `backspace` emits 0x08
25	    backarrow_key_mode: bool = false,
26	
27	    /// Terminal DEC mode 1035
28	    ignore_keypad_with_numlock: bool = false,
29	
30	    /// Terminal DEC mode 1036
31	    alt_esc_prefix: bool = false,
32	
33	    /// xterm "modifyOtherKeys mode 2". Details here:
34	    /// https://invisible-island.net/xterm/modified-keys.html
35	    modify_other_keys_state_2: bool = false,
36	
37	    /// Kitty keyboard protocol flags.
38	    kitty_flags: KittyFlags = .disabled,
39	
40	    /// Determines whether the "option" key on macOS is treated
41	    /// as "alt" or not. See the Ghostty `macos_option-as-alt` config
42	    /// docs for a more detailed description of why this is needed.
43	    macos_option_as_alt: OptionAsAlt = .false,
44	
45	    pub const default: Options = .{
46	        .cursor_key_application = false,
47	        .keypad_key_application = false,
48	        .ignore_keypad_with_numlock = false,
49	        .alt_esc_prefix = false,
50	        .modify_other_keys_state_2 = false,
51	        .kitty_flags = .disabled,
52	        .macos_option_as_alt = .false,
53	    };
54	
55	    /// Initialize our options from the terminal state.
56	    ///
57	    /// Note that `macos_option_as_alt` cannot be determined from
58	    /// terminal state so it must be set manually after this call.
59	    pub fn fromTerminal(t: *const Terminal) Options {
60	        return .{
61	            .alt_esc_prefix = t.modes.get(.alt_esc_prefix),
62	            .cursor_key_application = t.modes.get(.cursor_keys),
63	            .keypad_key_application = t.modes.get(.keypad_keys),
64	            .backarrow_key_mode = t.modes.get(.backarrow_key_mode),
65	            .ignore_keypad_with_numlock = t.modes.get(.ignore_keypad_with_numlock),
66	            .modify_other_keys_state_2 = t.flags.modify_other_keys_2,
67	            .kitty_flags = t.screens.active.kitty_keyboard.current(),
68	
69	            // These can't be known from the terminal state.
70	            .macos_option_as_alt = .false,
71	        };
72	    }
73	};
74	
75	/// Encode the key event to the writer in the proper format given
76	/// the options. For example, this will properly encode a key press
77	/// such as "ctrl+A" to Kitty format if Kitty encoding is enabled.
78	///
79	/// Not all key events will result in output. It is up to the caller
80	/// to use a writer that can track whether any output was written if
81	/// they care about that.
82	pub fn encode(
83	    writer: *std.Io.Writer,
84	    event: key.KeyEvent,
85	    opts: Options,
86	) std.Io.Writer.Error!void {
87	    //std.log.warn("KEYENCODER event={} opts={}", .{ event, opts });
88	    return if (opts.kitty_flags.int() != 0) try kitty(
89	        writer,
90	        event,
91	        opts,
92	    ) else try legacy(
93	        writer,
94	        event,
95	        opts,
96	    );
97	}
100	fn kitty(
101	    writer: *std.Io.Writer,
102	    event: key.KeyEvent,
103	    opts: Options,
104	) std.Io.Writer.Error!void {
105	    // This should never happen but we'll check anyway.
106	    if (opts.kitty_flags.int() == 0) return try legacy(
107	        writer,
108	        event,
109	        opts,
110	    );
111	
112	    // We only processed "press" events unless report events is active
113	    if (event.action == .release) {
114	        if (!opts.kitty_flags.report_events) return;
115	
116	        // Enter, backspace, and tab do not report release events unless "report
117	        // all" is set
118	        if (!opts.kitty_flags.report_all) {
119	            switch (event.key) {
120	                .enter, .backspace, .tab => return,
121	                else => {},
122	            }
123	        }
124	    }
125	
126	    const all_mods = event.mods;
127	    const effective_mods = event.effectiveMods();
128	    const binding_mods = effective_mods.binding();
129	
130	    // Find the entry for this key in the kitty table.
131	    const entry_: ?KittyEntry = entry: {
132	        // Functional or predefined keys
133	        for (kitty_entries) |entry| {
134	            if (entry.key == event.key) break :entry entry;
135	        }
136	
137	        // Otherwise, we use our unicode codepoint from UTF8. We
138	        // always use the unshifted value.
139	        if (event.unshifted_codepoint > 0) {
140	            break :entry .{
141	                .key = event.key,
142	                .code = event.unshifted_codepoint,
143	                .final = 'u',
144	                .modifier = false,
145	            };
146	        }
147	
148	        break :entry null;
149	    };
150	
151	    preprocessing: {
152	        // When composing, the only keys sent are plain modifiers.
153	        if (event.composing) {
154	            if (entry_) |entry| {
155	                if (entry.modifier) break :preprocessing;
156	            }
157	
158	            return;
159	        }
160	
161	        // IME confirmation still sends an enter key so if we have enter
162	        // and UTF8 text we just send it directly since we assume that is
163	        // what's happening. See legacy()'s similar logic for more details
164	        // on how to verify this.
165	        if (event.utf8.len > 0) utf8: {
166	            switch (event.key) {
167	                else => {},
168	                inline .enter, .backspace => |tag| {
169	                    // See legacy for why we handle this way.
170	                    if (isControlUtf8(event.utf8)) break :utf8;
171	                    if (comptime tag == .backspace) return;
172	                    return try writer.writeAll(event.utf8);
173	                },
174	            }
175	        }
176	
177	        // If we're reporting all then we always send CSI sequences.
178	        if (!opts.kitty_flags.report_all) {
179	            // Quote:
180	            // The only exceptions are the Enter, Tab and Backspace keys which
181	            // still generate the same bytes as in legacy mode this is to allow the
182	            // user to type and execute commands in the shell such as reset after a
183	            // program that sets this mode crashes without clearing it.
184	            //
185	            // Quote ("report all" mode):
186	            // Note that all keys are reported as escape codes, including Enter,
187	            // Tab, Backspace etc.
188	            if (binding_mods.empty()) {
189	                switch (event.key) {
190	                    .enter => return try writer.writeByte('\r'),
191	                    .tab => return try writer.writeByte('\t'),
192	                    .backspace => return try writer.writeByte(0x7F),
193	                    else => {},
194	                }
195	            }
196	
197	            // Send plain-text non-modified text directly to the terminal.
198	            // We don't send release events because those are specially encoded.
199	            if (event.utf8.len > 0 and
200	                binding_mods.empty() and
201	                event.action != .release)
202	            plain_text: {
203	                // We only do this for printable characters. We should
204	                // inspect the real unicode codepoint properties here but
205	                // the real world issue is usually control characters.
206	                const view = std.unicode.Utf8View.init(event.utf8) catch {
207	                    // Invalid UTF-8 so let's fallback to encoding the
208	                    // key press as if it didn't produce UTF-8 text. I'm
209	                    // not sure what should happen here according to the spec,
210	                    // since it doesn't specify this behavior. Presumably
211	                    // this is a caller bug.
212	                    break :plain_text;
213	                };
214	                var it = view.iterator();
215	                while (it.nextCodepoint()) |cp| {
216	                    if (isControl(cp)) break :plain_text;
217	                }
218	
219	                return try writer.writeAll(event.utf8);
220	            }
221	        }
222	    }
223	
224	    const entry = entry_ orelse {
225	        // No entry found. If we have UTF-8 text this is a pure text event
226	        // (e.g. composed/IME text), so send it as-is so programs can
227	        // still receive it. Release events never insert text, same as the
228	        // plain-text path above.
229	        if (event.action == .release) return;
230	        if (event.utf8.len == 0) return;
231	        return try writer.writeAll(event.utf8);
232	    };
233	
234	    // If this is just a modifier we require "report all" to send the sequence.
235	    if (entry.modifier and !opts.kitty_flags.report_all) return;
236	
237	    const seq: KittySequence = seq: {
238	        var seq: KittySequence = .{
239	            .key = entry.code,
240	            .final = entry.final,
241	            .mods = .fromInput(
242	                event.action,
243	                event.key,
244	                all_mods,
245	            ),
246	        };
247	
248	        if (opts.kitty_flags.report_events) {
249	            seq.event = switch (event.action) {
250	                .press => .press,
251	                .release => .release,
252	                .repeat => .repeat,
253	            };
254	        }
255	
256	        if (opts.kitty_flags.report_alternates) alternates: {
257	            // Break early if this is a control key
258	            if (isControl(seq.key)) break :alternates;
259	
260	            const view = std.unicode.Utf8View.init(event.utf8) catch {
261	                // Assume invalid UTF-8 means no UTF-8.
262	                break :alternates;
263	            };
264	            var it = view.iterator();
265	
266	            // If we have a codepoint in our UTF-8 sequence, then we can
267	            // report the shifted version.
268	            if (it.nextCodepoint()) |cp1| {
269	                // Set the first alternate (shifted version)
270	                if (cp1 != seq.key and seq.mods.shift) seq.alternates[0] = cp1;
271	
272	                // We want to know if there are additional codepoints because
273	                // our logic below depends on the utf8 being a single codepoint.
274	                const has_cp2 = it.nextCodepoint() != null;
275	
276	                // Set the base layout key. We only report this if this codepoint
277	                // differs from our pressed key.
278	                if (event.key.codepoint()) |base| {
279	                    if (base != seq.key and
280	                        (cp1 != base and !has_cp2))
281	                    {
282	                        seq.alternates[1] = base;
283	                    }
284	                }
285	            } else {
286	                // No UTF-8 so we can't report a shifted key but we can still
287	                // report a base layout key.
288	                if (event.key.codepoint()) |base| {
289	                    if (base != seq.key) seq.alternates[1] = base;
290	                }
291	            }
292	        }
293	
294	        if (opts.kitty_flags.report_associated and
295	            seq.event != .release)
296	        associated: {
297	            // Determine if the Alt modifier should be treated as an actual
298	            // modifier (in which case it prevents associated text) or as
299	            // the macOS Option key, which does not prevent associated text.
300	            const alt_prevents_text = if (comptime builtin.target.os.tag == .macos)
301	                switch (opts.macos_option_as_alt) {
302	                    .left => all_mods.sides.alt == .left,
303	                    .right => all_mods.sides.alt == .right,
304	                    .true => true,
305	                    .false => false,
306	                }
307	            else
308	                true;
309	
310	            if (seq.mods.preventsText(alt_prevents_text)) break :associated;
311	
312	            seq.text = event.utf8;
313	        }
314	
315	        break :seq seq;
316	    };
317	
318	    return try seq.encode(writer);
319	}
320	
321	/// Perform legacy encoding of the key event. "Legacy" in this case
322	/// is referring to the behavior of traditional terminals, plus
323	/// xterm's `modifyOtherKeys`, plus Paul Evans's "fixterms" spec.
324	/// These together combine the legacy protocol because they're all
325	/// meant to be extensions that do not change any existing behavior
326	/// and therefore safe to combine.
327	fn legacy(
328	    writer: *std.Io.Writer,
329	    event: key.KeyEvent,
330	    opts: Options,
331	) std.Io.Writer.Error!void {
332	    const all_mods = event.mods;
333	    const effective_mods = event.effectiveMods();
334	    const binding_mods = effective_mods.binding();
335	
336	    // Legacy encoding only does press/repeat
337	    if (event.action != .press and event.action != .repeat) return;
338	
339	    // If we're in a dead key state then we never emit a sequence.
340	    if (event.composing) return;
341	
342	    // If we match a PC style function key then that is our result.
343	    if (pcStyleFunctionKey(
344	        event.key,
345	        all_mods,
346	        opts.cursor_key_application,
347	        opts.keypad_key_application,
348	        opts.ignore_keypad_with_numlock,
349	        opts.modify_other_keys_state_2,
350	        opts.backarrow_key_mode,
351	    )) |sequence| pc_style: {
352	        // If we have UTF-8 text, then we never emit PC style function
353	        // keys. Many function keys (escape, enter, backspace) have
354	        // a specific meaning when dead keys are active and so we don't
355	        // want to send that to the terminal. Examples:
356	        //
357	        //   - Japanese: escape clears the dead key state
358	        //   - Korean: escape commits the dead key state
359	        //   - Korean: backspace should delete a single preedit char
360	        //
361	        if (event.utf8.len > 0) utf8: {
362	            switch (event.key) {
363	                else => {},
364	                inline .backspace, .enter, .escape => |tag| {
365	                    // We want to ignore control characters. This is because
366	                    // some apprts (macOS) will send control characters as
367	                    // UTF-8 encodings and we handle that manually.
368	                    if (isControlUtf8(event.utf8)) break :utf8;
369	
370	                    // Backspace encodes nothing because we modified IME.
371	                    // Enter/escape don't encode the PC-style encoding
372	                    // because we want to encode committed text.
373	                    if (comptime tag == .backspace) return;
374	                    break :pc_style;
375	                },
376	            }
377	        }
378	
379	        return try writer.writeAll(sequence);
380	    }
381	
382	    // In modify other keys state 2, we send the CSI 27 sequence
383	    // for any char with a modifier. This must happen before converting
384	    // Ctrl+<char> to a C0 byte because mode 2 encodes those keys, too.
385	    const utf8 = event.utf8;
386	    if (opts.modify_other_keys_state_2) modify_other: {
387	        const view = std.unicode.Utf8View.init(utf8) catch {
388	            // Assume invalid UTF-8 means we no UTF-8.
389	            break :modify_other;
390	        };
391	        var it = view.iterator();
392	        const codepoint = it.nextCodepoint() orelse break :modify_other;
393	
394	        // We only do this if we have a single codepoint. There shouldn't
395	        // ever be a multi-codepoint sequence that triggers this.
396	        if (it.nextCodepoint() != null) break :modify_other;
397	
398	        // The mods we encode for this are just the binding mods (shift, ctrl,
399	        // super, alt unless it is actually option).
400	        const mods = mods: {
401	            var mods_binding = event.mods.binding();
402	            if (comptime builtin.target.os.tag.isDarwin()) alt: {
403	                switch (opts.macos_option_as_alt) {
404	                    .false => {},
405	                    .true => break :alt,
406	                    .left => if (event.mods.sides.alt == .left) break :alt,
407	                    .right => if (event.mods.sides.alt == .right) break :alt,
408	                }
409	                mods_binding.alt = false;
410	            }
411	            break :mods mods_binding;
412	        };
413	
414	        // This copies xterm's `ModifyOtherKeys` function that returns
415	        // whether modify other keys should be encoded for the given
416	        // input.
417	        const should_modify = should_modify: {
418	            // xterm IsControlInput
419	            if (codepoint >= 0x40 and codepoint <= 0x7F)
420	                break :should_modify true;
421	
422	            // If we have anything other than shift pressed, encode.
423	            var mods_no_shift = mods;
424	            mods_no_shift.shift = false;
425	            if (!mods_no_shift.empty()) break :should_modify true;
426	
427	            // We only have shift pressed. We only allow space.
428	            if (codepoint == ' ') break :should_modify true;
429	
430	            // This logic isn't complete but I don't fully understand
431	            // the rest so I'm going to wait until we can have a
432	            // reasonable test scenario.
433	            break :should_modify false;
434	        };
435	
436	        if (should_modify) {
437	            for (function_keys.modifiers, 2..) |modset, code| {
438	                if (!mods.equal(modset)) continue;
439	                return try writer.print(
440	                    "\x1B[27;{};{}~",
441	                    .{ code, codepoint },
442	                );
443	            }
444	        }
445	    }
446	
447	    // If we match a control sequence, we output that directly. For
448	    // ctrlSeq we have to use all mods because we want it to only
449	    // match ctrl+<char>.
450	    if (ctrlSeq(
451	        event.key,
452	        event.utf8,
453	        event.unshifted_codepoint,
454	        all_mods,
455	    )) |char| {
456	        // C0 sequences support alt-as-esc prefixing.
457	        if (binding_mods.alt) {
458	            try writer.writeByte(0x1B);
459	            try writer.writeByte(char);
460	            return;
461	        }
462	
463	        try writer.writeByte(char);
464	        return;
465	    }
466	
467	    // If we have no UTF8 text then the only possibility is the
468	    // alt-prefix handling of unshifted codepoints... so we process that.
469	    if (utf8.len == 0) {
470	        _ = try legacyAltPrefix(
471	            writer,
472	            event,
473	            binding_mods,
474	            all_mods,
475	            opts,
476	        );
477	        return;
478	    }
479	
480	    // Let's see if we should apply fixterms to this codepoint.
481	    // At this stage of key processing, we only need to apply fixterms
482	    // to unicode codepoints if we have ctrl set.
483	    if (event.mods.ctrl) csiu: {
484	        // Important: we want to use the original mods here, not the
485	        // effective mods. The fixterms spec states the shifted chars
486	        // should be sent uppercase but Kitty changes that behavior
487	        // so we'll send all the mods.
488	        const csi_u_mods, const char = mods: {
489	            var mods = CsiUMods.fromInput(event.mods);
490	
491	            // Get our codepoint. If we have more than one codepoint this
492	            // can't be valid CSIu.
493	            const view = std.unicode.Utf8View.init(event.utf8) catch break :csiu;
494	            var it = view.iterator();
495	            var char = it.nextCodepoint() orelse break :csiu;
496	            if (it.nextCodepoint() != null) break :csiu;
497	
498	            // If our character is A to Z and we have shift set, then
499	            // we lowercase it. This is a Kitty-specific behavior that
500	            // we choose to follow and diverge from the fixterms spec.
501	            // This makes it easier for programs to detect shifted letters
502	            // for keybindings and is not just theoretical but used by
503	            // real programs.
504	            if (char >= 'A' and char <= 'Z' and mods.shift) {
505	                // We want to rely on apprt to send us the correct
506	                // unshifted codepoint...
507	                char = @intCast(std.ascii.toLower(@intCast(char)));
508	            }
509	
510	            // If our unshifted codepoint is identical to the shifted
511	            // then we consider shift. Otherwise, we do not because the
512	            // shift key was used to obtain the character. This is specified
513	            // by fixterms.
514	            if (event.unshifted_codepoint != char) {
515	                mods.shift = false;
516	            }
517	
518	            break :mods .{ mods, char };
519	        };
520	        return try writer.print(
521	            "\x1B[{};{}u",
522	            .{ char, csi_u_mods.seqInt() },
523	        );
524	    }
525	
526	    // If we have alt-pressed and alt-esc-prefix is enabled, then
527	    // we need to prefix the utf8 sequence with an esc.
528	    if (try legacyAltPrefix(
529	        writer,
530	        event,
531	        binding_mods,
532	        all_mods,
533	        opts,
534	    )) return;
535	
536	    // If we are on macOS, command+keys do not encode text. It isn't
537	    // typical for command+keys on macOS to ever encode text. They
538	    // don't in native text inputs (i.e. TextEdit) and they also don't
539	    // in other native terminals (Terminal.app officially but also
540	    // iTerm2).
541	    //
542	    // For Linux, we continue to encode text because it is typical.
543	    // For example on Gnome Console Super+b will encode a "b" character
544	    // with legacy encoding.
545	    if ((comptime builtin.target.os.tag == .macos) and all_mods.super) {
546	        return;
547	    }
548	
549	    return try writer.writeAll(utf8);
550	}
551	
552	fn legacyAltPrefix(
553	    writer: *std.Io.Writer,
554	    event: key.KeyEvent,
555	    binding_mods: key.Mods,
556	    mods: key.Mods,
557	    opts: Options,
558	) std.Io.Writer.Error!bool {
559	    // This only takes effect with alt pressed
560	    if (!binding_mods.alt or !opts.alt_esc_prefix) return false;
561	
562	    // On macOS, we only handle option like alt in certain
563	    // circumstances. Otherwise, macOS does a unicode translation
564	    // and we allow that to happen.
565	    if (comptime builtin.target.os.tag == .macos) {
566	        switch (opts.macos_option_as_alt) {
567	            .false => return false,
568	            .left => if (mods.sides.alt == .right) return false,
569	            .right => if (mods.sides.alt == .left) return false,
570	            .true => {},
571	        }
572	    }
573	
574	    // A single byte is already the exact text we want to prefix. In
575	    // particular, this preserves shifted ASCII punctuation.
576	    const utf8 = event.utf8;
577	    if (utf8.len == 1) {
578	        try writer.writeByte(0x1B);
579	        try writer.writeAll(utf8);
580	        return true;
581	    }
582	
583	    var unshifted_buf: [4]u8 = undefined;
584	    const value: []const u8 = value: {
585	        // On macOS, Option may translate the text into a different Unicode
586	        // value. When Option is configured as Alt, use the physical key's
587	        // unshifted codepoint just as the single-byte implementation did.
588	        if (comptime builtin.target.os.tag == .macos) {
589	            if (event.unshifted_codepoint > 0) {
590	                const len = std.unicode.utf8Encode(
591	                    event.unshifted_codepoint,
592	                    &unshifted_buf,
593	                ) catch return false;
594	                break :value unshifted_buf[0..len];
595	            }
596	        }
597	
598	        // Outside of the macOS translation case, prefix the complete UTF-8
599	        // text rather than truncating it to a single byte.
600	        if (utf8.len > 0) break :value utf8;
601	
602	        // Frontends may omit UTF-8 while still supplying the physical key's
603	        // unshifted codepoint. Encode the complete scalar in that case.
604	        if (event.unshifted_codepoint > 0) {
605	            const len = std.unicode.utf8Encode(
606	                event.unshifted_codepoint,
607	                &unshifted_buf,
608	            ) catch return false;
609	            break :value unshifted_buf[0..len];
610	        }
611	
612	        return false;
613	    };
614	
615	    try writer.writeByte(0x1B);
616	    try writer.writeAll(value);
617	    return true;
618	}
619	
631	fn pcStyleFunctionKey(
632	    keyval: key.Key,
633	    mods: key.Mods,
634	    cursor_key_application: bool,
635	    keypad_key_application_req: bool,
636	    ignore_keypad_with_numlock: bool,
637	    modify_other_keys: bool, // True if state 2
638	    backarrow_key_mode: bool,
639	) ?[]const u8 {
640	    // We only want binding-sensitive mods because lock keys
641	    // and directional modifiers (left/right) don't matter for
642	    // pc-style function keys.
643	    const mods_int = mods.binding().int();
644	
645	    // Keypad application keymode isn't super straightforward.
646	    // On xterm, in VT220 mode, numlock alone is enough to trigger
647	    // application mode. But in more modern modes, numlock is
648	    // ignored by default via mode 1035 (default true). If mode
649	    // 1035 is on, we always are in numerical keypad mode. If
650	    // mode 1035 is off, we are in application mode if the
651	    // proper numlock state is pressed. The numlock state is implicitly
652	    // determined based on the keycode sent (i.e. 1 with numlock
653	    // on will be kp_end).
654	    const keypad_key_application = keypad: {
655	        // If we're ignoring keypad then this is always false.
656	        // In other words, we're always in numerical keypad mode.
657	        if (ignore_keypad_with_numlock) break :keypad false;
658	
659	        // If we're not ignoring then we enable the desired state.
660	        break :keypad keypad_key_application_req;
661	    };
662	
663	    for (function_keys.keys.get(keyval)) |entry| {
664	        switch (entry.cursor) {
665	            .any => {},
666	            .normal => if (cursor_key_application) continue,
667	            .application => if (!cursor_key_application) continue,
668	        }
669	
670	        switch (entry.keypad) {
671	            .any => {},
672	            .normal => if (keypad_key_application) continue,
673	            .application => if (!keypad_key_application) continue,
674	        }
675	
676	        switch (entry.modify_other_keys) {
677	            .any => {},
678	            .set => if (modify_other_keys) continue,
679	            .set_other => if (!modify_other_keys) continue,
680	        }
681	
682	        const entry_mods_int = entry.mods.int();
683	        if (entry_mods_int == 0) {
684	            if (mods_int != 0 and !entry.mods_empty_is_any) continue;
685	            // mods are either empty, or empty means any so we allow it.
686	        } else if (entry_mods_int != mods_int) {
687	            // any set mods require an exact match
688	            continue;
689	        }
690	
691	        if (backarrow_key_mode)
692	            if (entry.sequence_decbkm) |sequence|
693	                return sequence;
694	
695	        return entry.sequence;
696	    }
697	
698	    return null;
699	}
700	
701	/// Returns the C0 byte for the key event if it should be used.
702	/// This converts a key event into the expected terminal behavior
703	/// such as Ctrl+C turning into 0x03, amongst many other translations.
1010	const KittySequence = struct {
1011	    key: u21,
1012	    final: u8,
1013	    mods: KittyMods = .{},
1014	    event: Event = .none,
1015	    alternates: [2]?u21 = .{ null, null },
1016	    text: []const u8 = "",
1017	
1018	    /// Values for the event code (see "event-type" in above comment).
1019	    /// Note that Kitty omits the ":1" for the press event but other
1020	    /// terminals include it. We'll include it.
1021	    const Event = enum(u2) {
1022	        none = 0,
1023	        press = 1,
1024	        repeat = 2,
1025	        release = 3,
1026	    };
1027	
1028	    pub fn encode(
1029	        self: KittySequence,
1030	        writer: *std.Io.Writer,
1031	    ) std.Io.Writer.Error!void {
1032	        if (self.final == 'u' or self.final == '~') return try self.encodeFull(writer);
1033	        return try self.encodeSpecial(writer);
1034	    }
1035	
1036	    fn encodeFull(
1037	        self: KittySequence,
1038	        writer: *std.Io.Writer,
1039	    ) std.Io.Writer.Error!void {
1040	        // Key section
1041	        try writer.print("\x1B[{d}", .{self.key});
1042	        // Write our alternates
1043	        if (self.alternates[0]) |shifted| try writer.print(":{d}", .{shifted});
1044	        if (self.alternates[1]) |base| {
1045	            if (self.alternates[0] == null) {
1046	                try writer.print("::{d}", .{base});
1047	            } else {
1048	                try writer.print(":{d}", .{base});
1049	            }
1050	        }
1051	
1052	        // Mods and events section
1053	        const mods = self.mods.seqInt();
1054	        var emit_prior = false;
1055	        if (self.event != .none and self.event != .press) {
1056	            try writer.print(
1057	                ";{d}:{d}",
1058	                .{ mods, @backingInt(self.event) },
1059	            );
1060	            emit_prior = true;
1061	        } else if (mods > 1) {
1062	            try writer.print(";{d}", .{mods});
1063	            emit_prior = true;
1064	        }
1065	
1066	        // Text section
1067	        if (self.text.len > 0) text: {
1068	            const view = std.unicode.Utf8View.init(self.text) catch {
1069	                // Assume invalid UTF-8 means we have no text.
1070	                break :text;
1071	            };
1072	            var it = view.iterator();
1073	            var count: usize = 0;
1074	            while (it.nextCodepoint()) |cp| {
1075	                // If the codepoint is non-printable ASCII character, skip.
1076	                if (isControl(cp)) continue;
1077	
1078	                // We need to add our ";". We need to add two if we didn't emit
1079	                // the modifier section. We only do this initially.
1080	                if (count == 0) {
1081	                    if (!emit_prior) try writer.writeByte(';');
1082	                    try writer.writeByte(';');
1083	                } else {
1084	                    try writer.writeByte(':');
1085	                }
1086	
1087	                try writer.print("{d}", .{cp});
1088	                count += 1;
1089	            }
1090	        }
1091	
1092	        try writer.print("{c}", .{self.final});
1093	    }
1094	
1095	    fn encodeSpecial(
1096	        self: KittySequence,
1097	        writer: *std.Io.Writer,
1098	    ) std.Io.Writer.Error!void {
1099	        const mods = self.mods.seqInt();
1100	        if (self.event != .none) {
1101	            return try writer.print("\x1B[1;{d}:{d}{c}", .{
1102	                mods,
1103	                @backingInt(self.event),
1104	                self.final,
1105	            });
1106	        }
1107	
1108	        if (mods > 1) {
1109	            return try writer.print("\x1B[1;{d}{c}", .{
1110	                mods,
1111	                self.final,
1112	            });
1113	        }
1114	
1115	        return try writer.print("\x1B[{c}", .{self.final});
1116	    }
1117	};
==== src/input/mouse_encode.zig ====
13	pub const Options = struct {
14	    /// Terminal mouse reporting mode (X10, normal, button, any).
15	    event: terminal.MouseEvent = .none,
16	
17	    /// Terminal mouse reporting format.
18	    format: terminal.MouseFormat = .x10,
19	
20	    /// Full renderer size used to convert surface-space pixel positions
21	    /// into grid cell coordinates (for most formats) and terminal-space
22	    /// pixel coordinates (for SGR-Pixels), as well as to determine
23	    /// whether a position falls outside the visible viewport.
24	    size: renderer_size.Size,
25	
26	    /// Whether any mouse button is currently pressed. When a motion
27	    /// event occurs outside the viewport, it is only reported if a
28	    /// button is held down and the event mode supports motion tracking.
29	    /// Without this, out-of-viewport motions are silently dropped.
30	    ///
31	    /// This should reflect the state of the current event as well, so
32	    /// if the encoded event is a button press, this should be true.
33	    any_button_pressed: bool = false,
34	
35	    /// Last reported viewport cell for motion deduplication.
36	    /// If null, motion deduplication state is not tracked.
37	    last_cell: ?*?point.Coordinate = null,
38	
39	    /// Initialize from terminal and renderer state. The caller may still
40	    /// set any_button_pressed and last_cell on the returned value.
41	    pub fn fromTerminal(
42	        t: *const Terminal,
43	        size: renderer_size.Size,
44	    ) Options {
45	        return .{
46	            .event = t.flags.mouse_event,
47	            .format = t.flags.mouse_format,
48	            .size = size,
49	        };
50	    }
51	};
52	
53	/// A normalized mouse event for protocol encoding.
54	pub const Event = struct {
55	    /// The action of this mouse event.
56	    action: mouse.Action = .press,
57	
58	    /// The button involved in this event. This can be null in the
59	    /// case of a motion action with no pressed buttons.
60	    button: ?mouse.Button = null,
61	
62	    /// Keyboard modifiers held during this event.
63	    mods: key.Mods = .{},
64	
65	    /// Mouse position in terminal-space pixels, with (0, 0) at the top-left
66	    /// of the terminal. Negative values are allowed and indicate positions
67	    /// above or to the left of the terminal. Values larger than the terminal
68	    /// size are also allowed and indicate right or below the terminal.
69	    pos: Pos = .{},
70	
71	    /// Mouse position in surface-space pixels.
72	    pub const Pos = extern struct {
73	        x: f32 = 0,
74	        y: f32 = 0,
75	    };
76	};
77	
78	/// Encode the mouse event to the writer according to the options.
79	///
80	/// Not all events result in output.
81	pub fn encode(
82	    writer: *std.Io.Writer,
83	    event: Event,
84	    opts: Options,
85	) std.Io.Writer.Error!void {
86	    if (!shouldReport(event, opts)) return;
87	
88	    // Handle scenarios where the mouse position is outside the viewport.
89	    // We always report release events no matter where they happen.
90	    if (event.action != .release and
91	        posOutOfViewport(event.pos, opts.size))
92	    {
93	        // If we don't have a motion-tracking event mode, do nothing,
94	        // because events outside the viewport are never reported in
95	        // such cases.
96	        if (!terminal.mouse.eventSendsMotion(opts.event)) return;
97	
98	        // For motion modes, we only report if a button is currently pressed.
99	        // This lets a TUI detect a click over the surface + drag out
100	        // of the surface.
101	        if (!opts.any_button_pressed) return;
102	    }
103	
104	    const cell = posToCell(event.pos, opts.size);
105	
106	    // We only send motion events when the cell changed unless
107	    // we're tracking raw pixels.
108	    if (event.action == .motion and opts.format != .sgr_pixels) {
109	        if (opts.last_cell) |last| {
110	            if (last.*) |last_cell| {
111	                if (last_cell.eql(cell)) return;
112	            }
113	        }
114	    }
115	
116	    // Update the last reported cell if we are tracking it.
117	    if (opts.last_cell) |last| last.* = cell;
118	
119	    const button_code = buttonCode(event, opts) orelse return;
120	    switch (opts.format) {
121	        .x10 => {
122	            if (cell.x > 222 or cell.y > 222) {
123	                log.info("X10 mouse format can only encode X/Y up to 223", .{});
124	                return;
125	            }
126	
127	            // + 1 because our x/y are zero-indexed and the protocol uses 1-indexing.
128	            try writer.writeAll("\x1B[M");
129	            try writer.writeByte(32 + button_code);
130	            try writer.writeByte(32 + @as(u8, @intCast(cell.x)) + 1);
131	            try writer.writeByte(32 + @as(u8, @intCast(cell.y)) + 1);
132	        },
133	
134	        .utf8 => {
135	            try writer.writeAll("\x1B[M");
136	            try writer.printUnicodeCodepoint(32 + @as(u21, button_code));
137	
138	            var buf: [4]u8 = undefined;
139	            const x_cp: u21 = @intCast(@as(u32, cell.x) + 33);
140	            const y_cp: u21 = @intCast(cell.y + 33);
141	
142	            const x_len = std.unicode.utf8Encode(x_cp, &buf) catch unreachable;
143	            try writer.writeAll(buf[0..x_len]);
144	
145	            const y_len = std.unicode.utf8Encode(y_cp, &buf) catch unreachable;
146	            try writer.writeAll(buf[0..y_len]);
147	        },
148	
149	        .sgr => try writer.print("\x1B[<{d};{d};{d}{c}", .{
150	            button_code,
151	            cell.x + 1,
152	            cell.y + 1,
153	            @as(u8, if (event.action == .release) 'm' else 'M'),
154	        }),
155	
156	        .urxvt => try writer.print("\x1B[{d};{d};{d}M", .{
157	            32 + button_code,
158	            cell.x + 1,
159	            cell.y + 1,
160	        }),
161	
162	        .sgr_pixels => {
163	            const pixels = posToPixels(event.pos, opts.size);
164	            try writer.print("\x1B[<{d};{d};{d}{c}", .{
165	                button_code,
166	                pixels.x,
167	                pixels.y,
168	                @as(u8, if (event.action == .release) 'm' else 'M'),
169	            });
170	        },
171	    }
172	}
173	
174	/// Returns true if this event should be reported for the given mouse
175	/// event mode.
176	fn shouldReport(event: Event, opts: Options) bool {
177	    return switch (opts.event) {
178	        .none => false,
179	
180	        // X10 only reports button presses of left, middle, and right.
181	        .x10 => event.action == .press and
182	            event.button != null and
183	            (event.button.? == .left or
184	                event.button.? == .middle or
185	                event.button.? == .right),
186	
187	        // Normal mode does not report motion.
188	        .normal => event.action != .motion,
189	
190	        // Button mode requires an active button for motion events.
191	        .button => event.button != null,
192	
193	        // Any mode reports everything.
194	        .any => true,
195	    };
196	}
197	
198	fn buttonCode(event: Event, opts: Options) ?u8 {
199	    var acc: u8 = code: {
200	        if (event.button == null) {
201	            // Null button means motion with no pressed button.
202	            break :code 3;
203	        }
204	
205	        if (event.action == .release and
206	            opts.format != .sgr and
207	            opts.format != .sgr_pixels)
208	        {
209	            // Legacy releases are always encoded as button 3.
210	            break :code 3;
211	        }
212	
213	        break :code switch (event.button.?) {
214	            .left => 0,
215	            .middle => 1,
216	            .right => 2,
217	            .four => 64,
218	            .five => 65,
219	            .six => 66,
220	            .seven => 67,
221	            .eight => 128,
222	            .nine => 129,
223	            else => return null,
224	        };
225	    };
226	
227	    // X10 does not include modifiers.
228	    if (opts.event != .x10) {
229	        if (event.mods.shift) acc += 4;
230	        if (event.mods.alt) acc += 8;
231	        if (event.mods.ctrl) acc += 16;
232	    }
233	
234	    // Motion adds another bit.
235	    if (event.action == .motion) acc += 32;
236	
237	    return acc;
238	}
239	
240	/// Terminal-space pixel position for SGR pixel reporting.
241	const PixelPoint = struct {
242	    x: i32,
243	    y: i32,
244	};
245	
246	/// Returns true if the surface-space pixel position is outside the
247	/// visible viewport bounds (negative or beyond screen dimensions).
248	fn posOutOfViewport(pos: Event.Pos, size: renderer_size.Size) bool {
249	    const max_x: f32 = @floatFromInt(size.screen.width);
250	    const max_y: f32 = @floatFromInt(size.screen.height);
251	    return pos.x < 0 or pos.y < 0 or pos.x > max_x or pos.y > max_y;
252	}
253	
254	/// Converts a surface-space pixel position to a zero-based grid cell
255	/// coordinate (column, row) within the terminal viewport. Out-of-bounds
256	/// values are clamped to the valid grid range (0 to columns/rows - 1).
257	fn posToCell(pos: Event.Pos, size: renderer_size.Size) point.Coordinate {
258	    const coord: renderer_size.Coordinate = .{ .surface = .{
259	        .x = @as(f64, @floatCast(pos.x)),
260	        .y = @as(f64, @floatCast(pos.y)),
261	    } };
262	    const grid = coord.convert(.grid, size).grid;
263	    return .{ .x = grid.x, .y = grid.y };
264	}
265	
266	/// Converts a surface-space pixel position to terminal-space pixel
267	/// coordinates (accounting for padding/scaling) used by SGR-Pixels mode.
268	/// Unlike grid conversion, terminal-space coordinates are not clamped
269	/// and may be negative or exceed the terminal dimensions.
270	fn posToPixels(pos: Event.Pos, size: renderer_size.Size) PixelPoint {
271	    const coord: renderer_size.Coordinate.Terminal = (renderer_size.Coordinate{ .surface = .{
272	        .x = @as(f64, @floatCast(pos.x)),
273	        .y = @as(f64, @floatCast(pos.y)),
274	    } }).convert(.terminal, size).terminal;
275	
276	    return .{
277	        .x = @as(i32, @intFromFloat(@round(coord.x))),
278	        .y = @as(i32, @intFromFloat(@round(coord.y))),
279	    };
280	}
==== src/input/paste.zig ====
4	/// The bracketed paste (mode 2004) frame written around the data.
5	pub const bracketed_prefix = "\x1b[200~";
6	pub const bracketed_suffix = "\x1b[201~";
7	
8	/// The maximum number of bytes `encode` adds around the data, so callers
9	/// can size a buffer for the full encoded result.
10	pub const max_frame_size = bracketed_prefix.len + bracketed_suffix.len;
11	
12	pub const Options = struct {
13	    /// True if bracketed paste mode is on.
14	    bracketed: bool,
15	
16	    /// Return the encoding options based on the current terminal state.
17	    pub fn fromTerminal(t: *const Terminal) Options {
18	        return .{
19	            .bracketed = t.modes.get(.bracketed_paste),
20	        };
21	    }
22	};
23	
24	/// Encode the given data for pasting. The resulting value can be written
25	/// to the pty to perform a paste of the input data.
26	///
27	/// The data can be either a `[]u8` or a `[]const u8`. If the data
28	/// type is const then `EncodeError` may be returned. If the data type
29	/// is mutable then this function can't return an error.
30	///
31	/// This slightly complex calling style allows for initially const
32	/// data to be passed in without an allocation, since it is rare in normal
33	/// use cases that the data will need to be modified. In the unlikely case
34	/// data does need to be modified, the caller can make a mutable copy
35	/// after seeing the error.
36	///
37	/// The data is returned as a set of slices to limit allocations. The caller
38	/// can combine the slices into a single buffer if desired.
39	///
40	/// WARNING: The input data is not checked for safety. See the `isSafe`
41	/// function to check if the data is safe to paste.
42	pub fn encode(
43	    data: anytype,
44	    opts: Options,
45	) switch (@TypeOf(data)) {
46	    []u8 => [3][]const u8,
47	    []const u8 => Error![3][]const u8,
48	    else => unreachable,
49	} {
50	    // These are the set of byte values that are always replaced by
51	    // a space (per xterm's behavior) for any text insertion method e.g.
52	    // a paste, drag and drop, etc. These are copied directly from xterm's
53	    // source.
54	    const strip: []const u8 = &.{
55	        0x00, // NUL
56	        0x08, // BS
57	        0x05, // ENQ
58	        0x04, // EOT
59	        0x1B, // ESC
60	        0x7F, // DEL
61	
62	        // These can be overridden by the running terminal program
63	        // via tcsetattr, so they aren't totally safe to hardcode like
64	        // this. In practice, I haven't seen modern programs change these
65	        // and its a much bigger architectural change to pass these through
66	        // so for now they're hardcoded.
67	        0x03, // VINTR (Ctrl+C)
68	        0x1C, // VQUIT (Ctrl+\)
69	        0x15, // VKILL (Ctrl+U)
70	        0x1A, // VSUSP (Ctrl+Z)
71	        0x11, // VSTART (Ctrl+Q)
72	        0x13, // VSTOP (Ctrl+S)
73	        0x17, // VWERASE (Ctrl+W)
74	        0x16, // VLNEXT (Ctrl+V)
75	        0x12, // VREPRINT (Ctrl+R)
76	        0x0F, // VDISCARD (Ctrl+O)
77	    };
78	
79	    const mutable = @TypeOf(data) == []u8;
80	
81	    var result: [3][]const u8 = .{ "", data, "" };
82	
83	    // If we have any of the strip values, then we need to replace them
84	    // with spaces. This is what xterm does and it does it regardless
85	    // of bracketed paste mode. This is a security measure to prevent pastes
86	    // from containing bytes that could be used to inject commands.
87	    if (std.mem.indexOfAny(u8, data, strip) != null) {
88	        if (comptime !mutable) return Error.MutableRequired;
89	        var offset: usize = 0;
90	        while (std.mem.indexOfAny(
91	            u8,
92	            data[offset..],
93	            strip,
94	        )) |idx| {
95	            offset += idx;
96	            data[offset] = ' ';
97	            offset += 1;
98	        }
99	    }
100	
101	    // Bracketed paste mode (mode 2004) wraps pasted data in
102	    // fenceposts so that the terminal can ignore things like newlines.
103	    if (opts.bracketed) {
104	        result[0] = bracketed_prefix;
105	        result[2] = bracketed_suffix;
106	        return result;
107	    }
108	
109	    // Non-bracketed. We have to replace newline with `\r`. This matches
110	    // the behavior of xterm and other terminals. For `\r\n` this will
111	    // result in `\r\r` which does match xterm.
112	    if (comptime mutable) {
113	        std.mem.replaceScalar(u8, data, '\n', '\r');
114	    } else if (std.mem.indexOfScalar(u8, data, '\n') != null) {
115	        return Error.MutableRequired;
116	    }
117	
118	    return result;
119	}
==== src/terminal/paste.zig ====
167	pub fn paste(ctx: Context, req: Request) Error!bool {
168	    // If the source is a clipboard and mode 5522 is enabled and
169	    // the caller can handle kitty events, then do a kitty event.
170	    if (req.source == .clipboard and
171	        ctx.terminal.modes.get(.kitty_paste_events))
172	    {
173	        if (ctx.kitty_clipboard) |kitty| {
174	            try pasteKittyEvent(ctx, kitty, req);
175	            return true;
176	        }
177	    }
214	fn pasteKittyEvent(
215	    ctx: Context,
216	    kitty: Context.KittyClipboard,
217	    req: Request,
218	) Error!void {
219	    const otp = try kitty_clipboard.generateOtp(kitty.io);
220	
221	    // Every representation is listed, never read. The listing is
222	    // bounded; a clipboard with more types than that is not a thing.
223	    var mimes_buf: [kitty_clipboard.max_listing_mimes][]const u8 = undefined;
224	    const mimes_len = @min(req.contents.len(), mimes_buf.len);
225	    for (mimes_buf[0..mimes_len], 0..) |*mime, i| mime.* = req.contents.mime(i);
226	
227	    // The grant is recorded before the event can reach the program,
228	    // and revoked if the event fails to be written so a failure never
229	    // leaves a grant for an event that was never sent. Using a
230	    // one-time grant consumes it, which is the revocation.
231	    try kitty.grants.grant(ctx.alloc, &otp, .read, true);
232	    errdefer _ = kitty.grants.use(ctx.alloc, &otp, .read);
233	
234	    try (kitty_clipboard.PasteEvent{
235	        // The protocol only distinguishes the clipboard from the
236	        // primary selection, so both non-standard locations report as
237	        // primary. Only a clipboard paste gets here; see `paste`.
238	        .primary = req.source.clipboard != .standard,
239	        .pw = &otp,
240	        .available = mimes_buf[0..mimes_len],
241	    }).encode(ctx.writer);
242	}
==== src/termio/mailbox.zig ====
47	    pub fn deinit(self: *Mailbox, alloc: Allocator) void {
48	        switch (self.*) {
49	            .spsc => |*v| {
50	                while (v.queue.pop(global.io())) |msg| msg.deinit();
51	                v.queue.destroy(alloc);
52	                v.wakeup.deinit();
53	            },
54	        }
55	    }
56	
57	    /// Sends the given message without notifying there are messages.
58	    ///
59	    /// If the optional mutex is given, it must already be LOCKED. If the
60	    /// send would block, we'll unlock this mutex, resend the message, and
61	    /// lock it again. This handles an edge case where queues are full.
62	    /// This may not apply to all writer types.
63	    pub fn send(
64	        self: *Mailbox,
65	        msg: termio.Message,
66	        mutex: ?*std.Io.Mutex,
67	    ) void {
68	        switch (self.*) {
69	            .spsc => |*mb| send: {
70	                // Try to write to the queue with an instant timeout. This is the
71	                // fast path because we can queue without a lock.
72	                if (mb.queue.push(global.io(), msg, .{ .instant = {} }) > 0) break :send;
73	
74	                // If we enter this conditional, the queue is full. We wake up
75	                // the writer thread so that it can process messages to clear up
76	                // space. However, the writer thread may require the renderer
77	                // lock so we need to unlock.
78	                mb.wakeup.notify() catch |err| {
79	                    log.warn("failed to wake up writer, data will be dropped err={}", .{err});
80	                    msg.deinit();
81	                    return;
82	                };
83	
84	                // Unlock the renderer state so the writer thread can acquire it.
85	                // Then try to queue our message before continuing. This is a very
86	                // slow path because we are having a lot of contention for data.
87	                // But this only gets triggered in certain pathological cases.
88	                //
89	                // Note that writes themselves don't require a lock, but there
90	                // are other messages in the writer queue (resize, focus) that
91	                // could acquire the lock. This is why we have to release our lock
92	                // here.
93	                if (mutex) |m| m.unlock(global.io());
94	                defer if (mutex) |m| m.lockUncancelable(global.io());
95	                if (mb.queue.push(global.io(), msg, .{ .forever = {} }) == 0) msg.deinit();
96	            },
97	        }
98	    }
99	
100	    /// Notify that there are new messages. This may be a noop depending
101	    /// on the writer type.
102	    pub fn notify(self: *Mailbox) void {
103	        switch (self.*) {
104	            .spsc => |*v| v.wakeup.notify() catch |err| {
105	                log.warn("failed to notify writer, data will be dropped err={}", .{err});
106	            },
107	        }
108	    }
==== macos/Sources/Ghostty/Surface View/SurfaceView_AppKit.swift ====
482	        override func sizeDidChange(_ size: CGSize) {
483	            // Ghostty wants to know the actual framebuffer size... It is very important
484	            // here that we use "size" and NOT the view frame. If we're in the middle of
485	            // an animation (i.e. a fullscreen animation), the frame will not yet be updated.
486	            // The size represents our final size we're going for.
487	            let scaledSize = self.convertToBacking(size)
488	            setSurfaceSize(width: UInt32(scaledSize.width), height: UInt32(scaledSize.height))
489	            // Store this size so we can reuse it when backing properties change
490	            contentSize = size
491	        }
492	
493	        private func setSurfaceSize(width: UInt32, height: UInt32) {
494	            guard let surface = self.surface else { return }
495	
496	            // Update our core surface
497	            ghostty_surface_set_size(surface, width, height)
498	
499	            // Update our cached size metrics
500	            let size = ghostty_surface_size(surface)
501	            DispatchQueue.main.async {
502	                // DispatchQueue required since this may be called by SwiftUI off
503	                // the main thread and Published changes need to be on the main
504	                // thread. This caused a crash on macOS <= 14.
505	                self.surfaceSize = size
506	            }
507	        }

==== src/terminal/search/Thread.zig ====
1	//! Search thread that handles searching a terminal for a string match.
2	//! This is expected to run on a dedicated thread to try to prevent too much
3	//! overhead to other terminal read/write operations.
4	//!
5	//! The current architecture of search does acquire global locks for accessing
6	//! terminal data, so there's still added contention, but we do our best to
7	//! minimize this by trading off memory usage (copying data to minimize lock
8	//! time).
9	pub const Thread = @This();
45	/// Allocator used for some state
46	alloc: std.mem.Allocator,
47	
48	/// The mailbox that can be used to send this thread messages. Note
49	/// this is a blocking queue so if it is full you will get errors (or block).
50	mailbox: *Mailbox,
51	
52	/// The event loop for the search thread.
53	loop: xev.Loop,
54	
55	/// This can be used to wake up the renderer and force a render safely from
56	/// any thread.
57	wakeup: xev.Async,
58	wakeup_c: xev.Completion = .{},
59	
60	/// This can be used to stop the thread on the next loop iteration.
61	stop: xev.Async,
62	stop_c: xev.Completion = .{},
63	
64	/// The timer used for refreshing the terminal state to determine if
65	/// we have a stale active area, viewport, screen change, etc. This is
66	/// CPU intensive so we stop doing this under certain conditions.
67	refresh: xev.Timer,
68	refresh_c: xev.Completion = .{},
69	refresh_active: bool = false,
70	
71	/// Search state. Starts as null and is populated when a search is
72	/// started (a needle is given).
73	search: ?TerminalSearch = null,
74	
75	/// The last-notified values used to diff search state into events.
76	notify_state: NotifyState = .{},
77	
78	/// The options used to initialize this thread.
79	opts: Options,
81	/// Initialize the thread. This does not START the thread. This only sets
82	/// up all the internal state necessary prior to starting the thread. It
83	/// is up to the caller to start the thread with the threadMain entrypoint.
84	pub fn init(alloc: Allocator, opts: Options) !Thread {
85	    // The mailbox for messaging this thread
86	    var mailbox = try Mailbox.create(alloc);
87	    errdefer mailbox.destroy(alloc);
88	
89	    // Create our event loop.
90	    var loop = try xev.Loop.init(.{});
91	    errdefer loop.deinit();
92	
93	    // This async handle is used to "wake up" the renderer and force a render.
94	    var wakeup_h = try xev.Async.init();
95	    errdefer wakeup_h.deinit();
96	
97	    // This async handle is used to stop the loop and force the thread to end.
98	    var stop_h = try xev.Async.init();
99	    errdefer stop_h.deinit();
100	
101	    // Refresh timer, see comments.
102	    var refresh_h = try xev.Timer.init();
103	    errdefer refresh_h.deinit();
104	
105	    return .{
106	        .alloc = alloc,
107	        .mailbox = mailbox,
108	        .loop = loop,
109	        .wakeup = wakeup_h,
110	        .stop = stop_h,
111	        .refresh = refresh_h,
112	        .opts = opts,
113	    };
114	}
115	
116	/// Clean up the thread. This is only safe to call once the thread
117	/// completes executing; the caller must join prior to this.
118	pub fn deinit(self: *Thread) void {
119	    self.refresh.deinit();
120	    self.wakeup.deinit();
121	    self.stop.deinit();
122	    self.loop.deinit();
123	    // Nothing can possibly access the mailbox anymore, destroy it.
124	    self.mailbox.destroy(self.alloc);
125	
126	    if (self.search) |*s| {
127	        self.opts.mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
128	        defer self.opts.mutex.unlock(global.io());
129	        s.deinit(self.opts.terminal);
130	    }
131	}
132	
133	/// The main entrypoint for the thread.
134	pub fn threadMain(self: *Thread) void {
135	    // Call child function so we can use errors...
136	    self.threadMain_() catch |err| {
137	        // In the future, we should expose this on the thread struct.
138	        log.warn("search thread err={}", .{err});
139	    };
140	}
141	
142	fn threadMain_(self: *Thread) !void {
143	    defer log.debug("search thread exited", .{});
144	
145	    // Right now, on Darwin, `std.Thread.setName` can only name the current
146	    // thread, and we have no way to get the current thread from within it,
147	    // so instead we use this code to name the thread instead.
148	    if (comptime builtin.target.os.tag.isDarwin()) {
149	        internal_os.macos.pthread_setname_np(&"search".*);
150	
151	        // We can run with lower priority than other threads.
152	        const class: internal_os.macos.QosClass = .utility;
153	        if (internal_os.macos.setQosClass(class)) {
154	            log.debug("thread QoS class set class={}", .{class});
155	        } else |err| {
156	            log.warn("error setting QoS class err={}", .{err});
157	        }
158	    }
159	
160	    // Start the async handlers
161	    self.wakeup.wait(&self.loop, &self.wakeup_c, Thread, self, wakeupCallback);
162	    self.stop.wait(&self.loop, &self.stop_c, Thread, self, stopCallback);
163	
164	    // Send an initial wakeup so we drain our mailbox immediately.
165	    try self.wakeup.notify();
166	
167	    // Start the refresh timer
168	    self.startRefreshTimer();
169	
170	    // Run
171	    log.debug("starting search thread", .{});
172	    defer {
173	        log.debug("starting search thread shutdown", .{});
174	
175	        // Send the quit message
176	        if (self.opts.event_cb) |cb| {
177	            cb(.quit, self.opts.event_userdata);
178	        }
179	    }
180	
181	    // Unlike some of our other threads, we interleave search work
182	    // with our xev loop so that we can try to make forward search progress
183	    // while also listening for messages.
184	    while (true) {
185	        // If our loop is canceled then we drain our messages and quit.
186	        if (self.loop.stopped()) {
187	            while (self.mailbox.pop(global.io())) |message| {
188	                log.debug("mailbox message ignored during shutdown={}", .{message});
189	            }
190	
191	            return;
192	        }
193	
194	        const s: *TerminalSearch = if (self.search) |*s| s else {
195	            // If we're not actively searching, we can block the loop
196	            // until it does some work.
197	            try self.loop.run(.once);
198	            continue;
199	        };
200	
201	        // If we have an active search, we always send any pending
202	        // notifications. Even if the search is complete, there may be
203	        // notifications to send.
204	        if (self.opts.event_cb) |cb| {
205	            self.notify(s, cb, self.opts.event_userdata);
206	        }
207	
208	        if (s.isComplete()) {
209	            // If our search is complete, there's no more work to do, we
210	            // can block until we have an xev action.
211	            try self.loop.run(.once);
212	            continue;
213	        }
214	
215	        // Tick the search. This will trigger any event callbacks, lock
216	        // for data loading, etc.
217	        switch (s.tick()) {
218	            // We're complete now when we were not before. Notify!
219	            .complete => {},
220	
221	            // Forward progress was made.
222	            .progress => {},
223	
224	            // All searches are blocked. Let's grab the lock and feed data.
225	            .blocked => {
226	                self.opts.mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
227	                defer self.opts.mutex.unlock(global.io());
228	                self.feedLocked(s);
229	            },
230	        }
231	
232	        // We have an active search, so we only want to process messages
233	        // we have but otherwise return immediately so we can continue the
234	        // search. If the above completed the search, we still want to
235	        // go around the loop as quickly as possible to send notifications,
236	        // and then we'll block on the loop next time.
237	        try self.loop.run(.no_wait);
238	    }
239	}
240	
241	/// Feed the search from the terminal state. The terminal mutex must
242	/// be held by the caller.
243	fn feedLocked(self: *Thread, s: *TerminalSearch) void {
244	    const t = self.opts.terminal;
245	
246	    // See the `search_viewport_dirty` flag on the terminal to know
247	    // what exactly this is for. But, if this is set, we know the renderer
248	    // found the viewport/active area dirty, so the active area must be
249	    // re-scanned.
250	    const active_dirty = t.flags.search_viewport_dirty;
251	    t.flags.search_viewport_dirty = false;
252	
253	    s.feed(t, active_dirty);
254	}
255	
256	/// Drain the mailbox.
257	fn drainMailbox(self: *Thread) !void {
258	    while (self.mailbox.pop(global.io())) |message| {
259	        log.debug("mailbox message={}", .{message});
260	        switch (message) {
261	            .change_needle => |v| {
262	                defer v.deinit();
263	                try self.changeNeedle(v.slice());
264	            },
265	            .select => |v| try self.select(v),
266	        }
267	    }
268	}
269	
270	fn select(self: *Thread, sel: ScreenSearch.Select) !void {
271	    const s = if (self.search) |*s| s else return;
272	
273	    self.opts.mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
274	    defer self.opts.mutex.unlock(global.io());
275	
276	    if (try s.select(self.opts.terminal, sel, .if_needed)) {
277	        // No matter what we reset our selected match cache. This will
278	        // trigger a callback which will trigger the renderer to wake up
279	        // so it can be notified the screen scrolled.
280	        self.notify_state.selected = null;
281	    }
282	}
283	
284	/// Change the search term to the given value.
285	fn changeNeedle(self: *Thread, needle: []const u8) !void {
286	    log.debug("changing search needle to '{s}'", .{needle});
287	
288	    // Stop the previous search
289	    if (self.search) |*s| {
290	        // If our search is unchanged, do nothing.
291	        if (std.ascii.eqlIgnoreCase(s.needle(), needle)) return;
292	
293	        {
294	            self.opts.mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
295	            defer self.opts.mutex.unlock(global.io());
296	            s.deinit(self.opts.terminal);
297	        }
298	        self.search = null;
299	        self.notify_state = .{};
300	
301	        // When the search changes then we need to emit that it stopped.
302	        if (self.opts.event_cb) |cb| {
303	            cb(
304	                .{ .total_matches = 0 },
305	                self.opts.event_userdata,
306	            );
307	            cb(
308	                .{ .selected_match = null },
309	                self.opts.event_userdata,
310	            );
311	            cb(
312	                .{ .viewport_matches = &.{} },
313	                self.opts.event_userdata,
314	            );
315	        }
316	    }
317	
318	    // No needle means stop the search.
319	    if (needle.len == 0) return;
320	
321	    // Setup our search state.
322	    self.search = try .init(self.alloc, needle);
323	    self.notify_state = .{};
324	
325	    // We need to grab the terminal lock and do an initial feed.
326	    self.opts.mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
327	    defer self.opts.mutex.unlock(global.io());
328	    self.feedLocked(&self.search.?);
329	}
330	
331	/// Notify about any changes to the search state by diffing against
332	/// the last-notified values.
333	///
334	/// This doesn't require any locking as it only reads search-owned state.
335	fn notify(
336	    self: *Thread,
337	    s: *TerminalSearch,
338	    cb: EventCallback,
339	    ud: ?*anyopaque,
340	) void {
341	    const state = &self.notify_state;
342	
343	    // A screen switch makes all previously notified per-screen state
344	    // stale, so reset it to force recalculations and notifications.
345	    if (state.key != s.active_key) {
346	        state.key = s.active_key;
347	        state.total = null;
348	        state.selected = null;
349	    }
350	
351	    const screen_search = s.activeScreenSearch() orelse return;
352	
353	    // Check our total match data
354	    const total = screen_search.matchesLen();
355	    if (total != state.total) {
356	        log.debug("notifying total matches={}", .{total});
357	        state.total = total;
358	        cb(.{ .total_matches = total }, ud);
359	    }
360	
361	    // Check our viewport matches. If they're stale, we collect them
362	    // now. We do this as part of notify and not tick because the
363	    // viewport search is very fast and doesn't require ticked progress
364	    // or feeds.
365	    if (s.stale_viewport_matches) viewport: {
366	        const matches = s.viewportMatches() catch |err| {
367	            log.warn("error collecting viewport matches err={}", .{err});
368	            break :viewport;
369	        };
370	
371	        log.debug("notifying viewport matches len={}", .{matches.len});
372	        cb(.{ .viewport_matches = matches }, ud);
373	    }
374	
375	    // Check our last selected match data.
376	    if (screen_search.selected) |m| match: {
377	        const flattened = screen_search.selectedMatch() orelse break :match;
378	        const untracked = flattened.untracked();
379	        if (state.selected) |prev| {
380	            if (prev.idx == m.idx and prev.highlight.eql(untracked)) {
381	                // Same selection, don't update it.
382	                break :match;
383	            }
384	        }
385	
386	        // New selection, notify!
387	        state.selected = .{
388	            .idx = m.idx,
389	            .highlight = untracked,
390	        };
391	
392	        log.debug("notifying selection updated idx={}", .{m.idx});
393	        cb(
394	            .{ .selected_match = .{
395	                .idx = m.idx,
396	                .highlight = flattened,
397	            } },
398	            ud,
399	        );
400	    } else if (state.selected != null) {
401	        log.debug("notifying selection cleared", .{});
402	        state.selected = null;
403	        cb(
404	            .{ .selected_match = null },
405	            ud,
406	        );
407	    }
408	
409	    // Send our complete notification if we just completed.
410	    if (!state.complete and s.isComplete()) {
411	        log.debug("notifying search complete", .{});
412	        state.complete = true;
413	        cb(.complete, ud);
414	    }
415	}
441	fn wakeupCallback(
442	    self_: ?*Thread,
443	    _: *xev.Loop,
444	    _: *xev.Completion,
445	    r: xev.Async.WaitError!void,
446	) xev.CallbackAction {
447	    _ = r catch |err| {
448	        log.warn("error in wakeup err={}", .{err});
449	        return .rearm;
450	    };
451	
452	    const self = self_.?;
453	
454	    // When we wake up, we drain the mailbox. Mailbox producers should
455	    // wake up our thread after publishing.
456	    self.drainMailbox() catch |err|
457	        log.warn("error draining mailbox err={}", .{err});
458	
459	    return .rearm;
460	}
461	
462	fn stopCallback(
463	    self_: ?*Thread,
464	    _: *xev.Loop,
465	    _: *xev.Completion,
466	    r: xev.Async.WaitError!void,
467	) xev.CallbackAction {
468	    _ = r catch unreachable;
469	    self_.?.loop.stop();
470	    return .disarm;
471	}

==== src/inspector/Inspector.zig ====
1	//! The Inspector is a development tool to debug the terminal. This is
2	//! useful for terminal application developers as well as people potentially
3	//! debugging issues in Ghostty itself.
4	const Inspector = @This();
58	pub fn init(alloc: Allocator) !Inspector {
59	    var gui: widgets.surface.Inspector = try .init(alloc);
60	    errdefer gui.deinit(alloc);
61	    return .{ .gui = gui };
62	}
63	
64	pub fn deinit(self: *Inspector, alloc: Allocator) void {
65	    self.gui.deinit(alloc);
66	}
67	
68	/// Returns the renderer info panel. This is a convenience function
69	/// to access and find this state to read and modify.
70	pub fn rendererInfo(self: *Inspector) *widgets.renderer.Info {
71	    return &self.gui.renderer_info;
72	}
73	
74	/// Record a keyboard event.
75	pub fn recordKeyEvent(
76	    self: *Inspector,
77	    alloc: Allocator,
78	    ev: inspector.KeyEvent,
79	) Allocator.Error!void {
80	    const max_capacity = 50;
81	
82	    const events: *widgets.key.EventRing = &self.gui.key_stream.events;
83	    events.append(ev) catch |err| switch (err) {
84	        error.OutOfMemory => if (events.capacity() < max_capacity) {
85	            // We're out of memory, but we can allocate to our capacity.
86	            const new_capacity = @min(events.capacity() * 2, max_capacity);
87	            try events.resize(alloc, new_capacity);
88	            try events.append(ev);
89	        } else {
90	            var it = events.iterator(.forward);
91	            if (it.next()) |old_ev| old_ev.deinit(alloc);
92	            events.deleteOldest(1);
93	            try events.append(ev);
94	        },
95	    };
96	}
97	
98	/// Record data read from the pty.
99	pub fn recordPtyRead(
100	    self: *Inspector,
101	    alloc: Allocator,
102	    t: *terminal.Terminal,
103	    data: []const u8,
104	) !void {
105	    try self.gui.vt_stream.recordPtyRead(
106	        alloc,
107	        t,
108	        data,
109	    );
110	}

152	        // Activate the inspector on the new surface.
153	        const surface = surface_ orelse return;
154	        const core_surface = surface.core() orelse return;
155	        core_surface.activateInspector() catch |err| {
156	            log.warn("failed to activate inspector err={}", .{err});
157	            return;
158	        };
159	
160	        // We use a weak reference on surface to determine if the surface
161	        // was closed while our inspector was active.
162	        surface.as(gobject.Object).weakRef(
163	            surfaceWeakNotify,
164	            self,
165	        );
166	
167	        // Store our surface. We don't need to ref this because we setup
168	        // the weak notify above.
169	        priv.surface = surface;
170	
171	        self.queueRender();
172	    }
177	    fn surfaceWeakNotify(
178	        ud: ?*anyopaque,
179	        surface: *gobject.Object,
180	    ) callconv(.c) void {
181	        const self: *Self = @ptrCast(@alignCast(ud orelse return));
182	        const priv = self.private();
183	
184	        // The weak notify docs call out that we can specifically use the
185	        // pointer values for comparison, but the objects themselves are unsafe.
186	        if (@intFromPtr(priv.surface) != @intFromPtr(surface)) return;
187	
188	        // According to weak notify docs, "surface" is in the "dispose" state.
189	        // Our surface doesn't clear the core surface until the "finalize"
190	        // state so we should be able to safely access it here. We need to
191	        // be really careful though.
192	        const old = priv.surface orelse return;
193	        const core_surface = old.core() orelse return;
194	        core_surface.deactivateInspector();
195	        priv.surface = null;
196	        self.as(gobject.Object).notifyByPspec(properties.surface.impl.param_spec);
==== src/termio/Exec.zig ====
59	pub fn deinit(self: *Exec) void {
60	    self.subprocess.deinit();
61	}
139	    // Start our read thread
140	    const read_thread = try std.Thread.spawn(
141	        .{},
142	        if (builtin.target.os.tag == .windows) ReadThread.threadMainWindows else ReadThread.threadMainPosix,
143	        .{ pty_fds.read, io, pipe[0] },
144	    );
145	    read_thread.setName(global.io(), "io-reader") catch {};
146	
147	    // Setup our threadata backend state to be our own
148	    td.backend = .{ .exec = .{
149	        .start = process_start,
150	        .write_stream = stream,
151	        .process = process,
152	        .read_thread = read_thread,
153	        .read_thread_pipe = pipe[1],
154	        .read_thread_fd = pty_fds.read,
155	        .termios_timer = termios_timer,
156	    } };
158	    // Start our process watcher. If we have an xev.Process use it.
159	    if (process) |*p| p.wait(
160	        td.loop,
161	        &td.backend.exec.process_wait_c,
162	        termio.Termio.ThreadData,
163	        td,
164	        processExit,
165	    ) else if (comptime build_config.flatpak) flatpak: {
166	        switch (self.subprocess.process orelse break :flatpak) {
167	            // If we're in flatpak and we have a flatpak command
168	            // then we can run the special flatpak logic for watching.
169	            .flatpak => |*c| c.waitXev(
170	                td.loop,
171	                &td.backend.exec.flatpak_wait_c,
172	                termio.Termio.ThreadData,
173	                td,
174	                flatpakExit,
175	            ),
176	
177	            .fork_exec => {},
178	        }
179	    }
196	pub fn threadExit(self: *Exec, td: *termio.Termio.ThreadData) void {
197	    assert(td.backend == .exec);
198	    const exec = &td.backend.exec;
199	
200	    if (exec.exited) self.subprocess.externalExit();
201	    self.subprocess.stop();
202	
203	    // Quit our read thread after exiting the subprocess so that
204	    // we don't get stuck waiting for data to stop flowing if it is
205	    // a particularly noisy process.
206	    switch (posix.errno(posix.system.write(exec.read_thread_pipe, "x", 1))) {
207	        .SUCCESS => {},
208	
209	        // EPIPE means that our read thread is closed already, which is
210	        // completely fine since that is what we were trying to achieve.
211	        .PIPE => {},
212	
213	        else => |e| log.warn(
214	            "error writing to read thread quit pipe err=E{s}",
215	            .{@tagName(e)},
216	        ),
217	    }
218	
219	    if (comptime builtin.target.os.tag == .windows) {
220	        // Interrupt the blocking read so the thread can see the quit message
221	        if (windows.exp.kernel32.CancelIoEx(exec.read_thread_fd, null) == windows.FALSE) {
222	            switch (windows.GetLastError()) {
223	                .NOT_FOUND => {},
224	                else => |err| log.warn("error interrupting read thread err={}", .{err}),
225	            }
226	        }
227	    }
228	
229	    exec.read_thread.join();
230	}
274	fn processExitCommon(td: *termio.Termio.ThreadData, exit_code: u32) void {
275	    assert(td.backend == .exec);
276	    const execdata = &td.backend.exec;
277	    execdata.exited = true;
278	
279	    // Determine how long the process was running for.
280	    const runtime_ms: u64 = @max(
281	        0,
282	        execdata.start.untilNow(global.io(), .awake).toMilliseconds(),
283	    );
284	    log.debug("child process exited status={} runtime={}ms", .{ exit_code, runtime_ms });
285	
286	    // We always notify the surface immediately that the child has
287	    // exited and some metadata about the exit.
288	    _ = td.surface_mailbox.push(.{
289	        .child_exited = .{
290	            .exit_code = exit_code,
291	            .runtime_ms = runtime_ms,
292	        },
293	    }, .{ .forever = {} });
294	}
295	
296	fn processExit(
297	    td_: ?*termio.Termio.ThreadData,
298	    _: *xev.Loop,
299	    _: *xev.Completion,
300	    r: xev.Process.WaitError!u32,
301	) xev.CallbackAction {
302	    const exit_code = r catch unreachable;
303	    processExitCommon(td_.?, exit_code);
304	    return .disarm;
305	}
