307	fn flatpakExit(
308	    td_: ?*termio.Termio.ThreadData,
309	    _: *xev.Loop,
310	    _: *FlatpakHostCommand.Completion,
311	    r: FlatpakHostCommand.WaitError!u8,
312	) void {
313	    const exit_code = r catch unreachable;
314	    processExitCommon(td_.?, exit_code);
315	}
549	    pub fn deinit(self: *ThreadData, alloc: Allocator) void {
550	        _ = posix.system.close(self.read_thread_pipe);
551	
552	        // Clear our write pool. We know we aren't ever going to do
553	        // any more IO since we stop our data stream below so we can just
554	        // drop this.
555	        self.write_pool.deinit(alloc);
556	
557	        // Stop our process watcher
558	        if (self.process) |*p| p.deinit();
559	
560	        // Stop our write stream
561	        self.write_stream.deinit();
562	
563	        // Stop our termios timer
564	        self.termios_timer.deinit();
565	    }
886	    /// Clean up the subprocess. This will stop the subprocess if it is started.
887	    pub fn deinit(self: *Subprocess) void {
888	        self.stop();
889	        if (self.pty) |*pty| pty.deinit();
890	        if (self.env) |*env| env.deinit();
891	        self.arena.deinit();
892	        self.* = undefined;
893	    }
1105	    /// Called to notify that we exited externally so we can unset our
1106	    /// running state.
1107	    pub fn externalExit(self: *Subprocess) void {
1108	        self.process = null;
1109	    }
1110	
1111	    /// Stop the subprocess. This is safe to call anytime. This will wait
1112	    /// for the subprocess to register that it has been signalled, but not
1113	    /// for it to terminate, so it will not block.
1114	    /// This does not close the pty.
1115	    pub fn stop(self: *Subprocess) void {
1116	        switch (self.process orelse return) {
1117	            .fork_exec => |*cmd| {
1118	                // Note: this will also wait for the command to exit, so
1119	                // DO NOT call cmd.wait
1120	                killCommand(cmd) catch |err|
1121	                    log.err("error sending SIGHUP to command, may hang: {}", .{err});
1122	            },
1123	
1124	            .flatpak => |*cmd| if (comptime build_config.flatpak) {
1125	                killCommandFlatpak(cmd) catch |err|
1126	                    log.err("error sending SIGHUP to command, may hang: {}", .{err});
1127	                _ = cmd.wait() catch |err|
1128	                    log.err("error waiting for command to exit: {}", .{err});
1129	            },
1130	        }
1131	
1132	        self.process = null;
1133	    }
1158	    /// Kill the underlying subprocess. This sends a SIGHUP to the child
1159	    /// process. This also waits for the command to exit and will return the
1160	    /// exit code.
1161	    fn killCommand(command: *Command) !void {
1162	        if (command.pid) |pid| {
1163	            switch (builtin.target.os.tag) {
1164	                .windows => {
1165	                    if (windows.exp.kernel32.TerminateProcess(pid, 0) == windows.FALSE) {
1166	                        return windows.unexpectedError(windows.GetLastError());
1167	                    }
1168	
1169	                    _ = try command.wait(false);
1170	                },
1171	
1172	                else => try killPid(pid),
1173	            }
1174	        }
1175	    }
1176	
1177	    fn killPid(pid: c.pid_t) !void {
1178	        const pgid = getpgid(pid) orelse return;
1179	
1180	        // It is possible to send a killpg between the time that
1181	        // our child process calls setsid but before or simultaneous
1182	        // to calling execve. In this case, the direct child dies
1183	        // but grandchildren survive. To work around this, we loop
1184	        // and repeatedly kill the process group until all
1185	        // descendents are well and truly dead. We will not rest
1186	        // until the entire family tree is obliterated.
1187	        while (true) {
1188	            switch (posix.errno(c.killpg(pgid, c.SIGHUP))) {
1189	                .SUCCESS => log.debug("process group killed pgid={}", .{pgid}),
1190	                else => |err| killpg: {
1191	                    if ((comptime builtin.target.os.tag.isDarwin()) and
1192	                        err == .PERM)
1193	                    {
1194	                        log.debug("killpg failed with EPERM, expected on Darwin and ignoring", .{});
1195	                        break :killpg;
1196	                    }
1197	
1198	                    log.warn("error killing process group pgid={} err={}", .{ pgid, err });
1199	                    return error.KillFailed;
1200	                },
1201	            }
1202	
1203	            // See Command.zig wait for why we specify WNOHANG.
1204	            // The gist is that it lets us detect when children
1205	            // are still alive without blocking so that we can
1206	            // kill them again.
1207	            const res_pid = while (true) {
1208	                const rc = posix.system.waitpid(pid, null, std.c.W.NOHANG);
1209	                if (posix.errno(rc) == .INTR) continue;
1210	                break rc;
1211	            };
1212	            log.debug("waitpid result={}", .{res_pid});
1213	            if (res_pid != 0) break;
1214	            try std.Io.sleep(global.io(), .fromMilliseconds(10), .awake);
1215	        }
1216	    }
1217	
1218	    fn getpgid(pid: c.pid_t) ?c.pid_t {
1219	        // Get our process group ID. Before the child pid calls setsid
1220	        // the pgid will be ours because we forked it. Its possible that
1221	        // we may be calling this before setsid if we are killing a surface
1222	        // VERY quickly after starting it.
1223	        const my_pgid = c.getpgid(0);
1224	
1225	        // We loop while pgid == my_pgid. The expectation if we have a valid
1226	        // pid is that setsid will eventually be called because it is the
1227	        // FIRST thing the child process does and as far as I can tell,
1228	        // setsid cannot fail. I'm sure that's not true, but I'd rather
1229	        // have a bug reported than defensively program against it now.
1230	        while (true) {
1231	            const pgid = c.getpgid(pid);
1232	            if (pgid == my_pgid) {
1233	                log.warn("pgid is our own, retrying", .{});
1234	                std.Io.sleep(global.io(), .fromMilliseconds(10), .awake) catch {};
1235	                continue;
1236	            }
1237	
1238	            // Don't know why it would be zero but its not a valid pid
1239	            if (pgid == 0) return null;
1240	
1241	            // If the pid doesn't exist then... we're done!
1242	            if (pgid == c.ESRCH) return null;
1243	
1244	            // If we have an error we're done.
1245	            if (pgid < 0) {
1246	                log.warn("error getting pgid for kill", .{});
1247	                return null;
1248	            }
1249	
1250	            return pgid;
1251	        }
1252	    }
1253	
1254	    /// Kill the underlying process started via Flatpak host command.
1255	    /// This sends a signal via the Flatpak API.
1256	    fn killCommandFlatpak(command: *FlatpakHostCommand) !void {
1257	        try command.signal(c.SIGHUP, true);
1258	    }
1462	        const gather_thread = std.Thread.spawn(
1463	            .{},
1464	            gatherMainPosix,
1465	            .{ fd, quit, &pipeline },
1466	        ) catch |err| {
1467	            // If we can't spawn a thread the process is already
1468	            // doomed (every surface spawns several), so don't try
1469	            // to limp along.
1470	            log.err("read thread exiting, failed to spawn gather thread err={}", .{err});
1471	            return;
1472	        };
1473	        defer gather_thread.join();
1474	        if (comptime !builtin.target.os.tag.isDarwin()) {
1475	            gather_thread.setName(global.io(), "io-gather") catch {};
1476	        }
1477	
1478	        // This thread is the parse stage. We consume batches in ring
1479	        // order until the gather stage reports the stream is over and
1480	        // the ring is drained.
1481	        while (true) {
1482	            const batch: []const u8 = batch: {
1483	                pipeline.mutex.lockUncancelable(global.io());
1484	                defer pipeline.mutex.unlock(global.io());
1485	                while (pipeline.count == 0) {
1486	                    if (pipeline.done) return;
1487	                    pipeline.batch_ready.waitUncancelable(global.io(), &pipeline.mutex);
1488	                }
1489	                const slot = pipeline.tail;
1490	                break :batch pipeline.bufs[slot][0..pipeline.lens[slot]];
1491	            };
1635	                        // On a quit signal we deliver what we have
1636	                        // and stop.
1637	                        if (pollfds[1].revents & posix.POLL.IN != 0) {
1638	                            log.info("read thread got quit signal", .{});
1639	                            fatal = true;
1640	                            break :gather;
1641	                        }
1713	            // Wait for data. The idle fd is sliced off: the parse
1714	            // stage only writes to it while we're bridging.
1715	            _ = posix.poll(pollfds[0..2], -1) catch |err| {
1716	                log.warn("poll failed on read thread, exiting early err={}", .{err});
1717	                return;
1718	            };
1719	
1720	            // If our quit fd is set, we're done.
1721	            if (pollfds[1].revents & posix.POLL.IN != 0) {
1722	                log.info("read thread got quit signal", .{});
1723	                return;
1724	            }
1725	
1726	            // If our pty fd is closed, then we're also done with our
1727	            // read thread.
1728	            if (pollfds[0].revents & posix.POLL.HUP != 0) {
1729	                log.info("pty fd closed, read thread exiting", .{});
1730	                return;
1731	            }
1732	        }
1733	    }
1778	    fn threadMainWindows(fd: posix.fd_t, io: *termio.Termio, quit: posix.fd_t) void {
1779	        // Always close our end of the pipe when we exit.
1780	        defer _ = posix.system.close(quit);
1781	
1782	        // Setup our crash metadata
1783	        crash.sentry.thread_state = .{
1784	            .type = .io,
1785	            .surface = io.surface_mailbox.surface,
1786	        };
1787	        defer crash.sentry.thread_state = null;
1788	
1789	        var buf: [1024]u8 = undefined;
1790	        while (true) {
1791	            while (true) {
1792	                var n: windows.DWORD = 0;
1793	                if (windows.exp.kernel32.ReadFile(fd, &buf, buf.len, &n, null) == windows.FALSE) {
1794	                    const err = windows.GetLastError();
1795	                    switch (err) {
1796	                        // Check for a quit signal
1797	                        .OPERATION_ABORTED => break,
1798	
1799	                        else => {
1800	                            log.err("io reader error err={}", .{err});
1801	                            unreachable;
1802	                        },
1803	                    }
1804	                }
1805	
1806	                @call(.always_inline, termio.Termio.processOutput, .{ io, buf[0..n] });
1807	
1808	                // See threadMainPosix: hand the renderer state mutex
1809	                // off if the renderer is waiting, since this loop
1810	                // would otherwise starve it under heavy output.
1811	                io.renderer_state.yieldToDemand(global.io());
1812	            }
1813	
1814	            var quit_bytes: windows.DWORD = 0;
1815	            if (windows.exp.kernel32.PeekNamedPipe(quit, null, 0, null, &quit_bytes, null) == windows.FALSE) {
1816	                const err = windows.GetLastError();
1817	                log.err("quit pipe reader error err={}", .{err});
1818	                unreachable;
1819	            }
1820	
1821	            if (quit_bytes > 0) {
1822	                log.info("read thread got quit signal", .{});
1823	                return;
1824	            }
1825	        }
1826	    }

186	/// A surface mailbox.

191	    /// Send a message to the surface.
192	    pub fn push(
193	        self: Mailbox,
194	        msg: Message,
195	        timeout: App.Mailbox.Queue.Timeout,
196	    ) App.Mailbox.Queue.Size {
197	        // Surface message sending is actually implemented on the app
198	        // thread, so we have to rewrap the message with our surface
199	        // pointer and send it to the app thread.
200	        return self.app.push(.{
201	            .surface_message = .{
202	                .surface = self.surface,
203	                .message = msg,
204	            },
205	        }, timeout);

1348	/// If true, keep the terminal open after the command exits. Normally, the
1349	/// terminal window closes when the running command (such as a shell) exits.
1350	/// With this true, the terminal window will stay open until any keypress is
1351	/// received.
1352	///
1353	/// This is primarily useful for scripts or debugging.

1356	/// The number of milliseconds of runtime below which we consider a process exit

1358	/// too quickly.
1359	///
1360	/// On Linux, this must be paired with a non-zero exit code. On macOS, we allow

1362	/// command.

==== src/apprt/gtk/class/surface_child_exited.zig ====
69	    pub fn setData(
70	        self: *Self,
71	        data_: ?*const apprt.surface.Message.ChildExited,
72	    ) void {
73	        const priv = self.private();

89	    fn propData(
90	        self: *Self,
91	        _: *gobject.ParamSpec,
92	        _: ?*anyopaque,
93	    ) callconv(.c) void {
94	        const priv = self.private();
95	        const banner = priv.banner;
96	        const data = priv.data orelse {

99	            return;

103	            self.as(gtk.Widget).addCssClass("normal");
104	            self.as(gtk.Widget).removeCssClass("abnormal");
105	        } else {

107	            self.as(gtk.Widget).removeCssClass("normal");
108	            self.as(gtk.Widget).addCssClass("abnormal");
109	        }

1855	            _ app: ghostty_app_t,
1856	            target: ghostty_target_s,

1858	        ) -> Bool {

1867	                surfaceView.setChildExitedMessage(.init(v, threshold: config.abnormalCommandExitRuntime))

1869	            default:

==== macos/Sources/Ghostty/Ghostty.ChildExitedMessage.swift ====
6	    struct ChildExitedMessage {
7	        enum Level {
8	            case success, error
9	        }
10	        let text: String
11	        let level: Level
12	
13	        init(_ message: ghostty_surface_message_childexited_s, threshold abnormalCommandExitRuntime: Duration) {
14	            var level: Level
15	            switch Int(message.exit_code) {
16	            case Int(EXIT_SUCCESS):
17	                level = .success
18	            default:
19	                level = .error
20	            }
21	            // See: Surface.zig/childExited
22	            // If our runtime was below some threshold then we assume that this
23	            // was an abnormal exit and we show an error message.
24	            if abnormalCommandExitRuntime >= .milliseconds(message.timetime_ms) {
25	                level = .error
26	                let measure = Measurement.init(value: Double(message.timetime_ms), unit: UnitDuration.milliseconds)
27	                let formatter = MeasurementFormatter()
28	                if message.timetime_ms > 1000 {
29	                    formatter.unitOptions = .naturalScale
30	                } else {
31	                    formatter.unitOptions = .providedUnit
32	                }
33	                formatter.locale = .init(identifier: "en_US")
34	                text = "Process exited after **`\(formatter.string(from: measure))`**. Press any key to close the terminal."
35	            } else {
36	                text = "Process exited. Press any key to close the terminal."
37	            }
38	            self.level = level
39	        }
40	    }

==== src/renderer/DisplayLink.zig ====
115	///
116	/// Must be called on the main thread. Stopping waits for CoreVideo's
117	/// IO thread to exit, and that is only safe on the main thread, where
118	/// CoreVideo's own stop and start also run and so cannot restart the
119	/// link while we wait. Our apprts always destroy surfaces on the main
120	/// thread.
121	///
122	/// The link is stopped before this returns, so the output callback is
123	/// not invoked again and whatever `userdata` pointed at can be freed.
124	///
125	/// No other method may be called on this link after or concurrently
126	/// with `close`. In practice the render thread that makes those calls
127	/// has already exited by the time the renderer is deinitialized.
128	pub fn close(self: *DisplayLink) void {
129	    assert(!self.closed);
130	    self.closed = true;
131	
132	    if (self.link.isRunning()) self.link.stop() catch |err| {
133	        log.warn("error stopping display link err={}", .{err});
134	    };
135	    self.link.release();
136	
137	    // If a sync is queued on the main thread it runs after we return
138	    // and still needs this struct, so it frees it instead of us.
139	    if (!self.sync_queued.load(.seq_cst)) self.destroy();
140	}
141	
142	fn destroy(self: *DisplayLink) void {
143	    const alloc = self.alloc;
144	    self.* = undefined;
145	    alloc.destroy(self);
146	}

460	        _ = windows.exp.kernel32.CloseHandle(self.out_pipe_pty);
461	        _ = windows.exp.kernel32.CloseHandle(self.out_pipe);
462	        _ = windows.exp.kernel32.ClosePseudoConsole(self.pseudo_console);
463	        self.* = undefined;
464	    }

==== src/terminal/SelectionGesture.zig ====
172	pub fn deinit(self: *SelectionGesture, t: *Terminal) void {
173	    // Grab our pagelist that is associated with the pin. If it doesn't
174	    // exist anymore then our tracked pin is already free.
175	    const pin = self.left_click_pin orelse return;
176	    if (t.screens.generation(self.left_click_screen) != self.left_click_screen_generation) return;
177	    const screen = t.screens.get(self.left_click_screen) orelse return;
178	    screen.pages.untrackPin(pin);
179	}

6332	        self.pool.pins.destroy(p);
6333	    }

==== src/font/SharedGridSet.zig ====
411	/// Decrement the ref count for the given key. If the ref count is zero,
412	/// the grid will be deinitialized and removed from the map.j:w
413	pub fn deref(self: *SharedGridSet, key: Key) void {
414	    self.lock.lockUncancelable(global.io());
415	    defer self.lock.unlock(global.io());
416	
417	    const entry = self.map.getEntry(key) orelse return;
418	    assert(entry.value_ptr.ref >= 1);
419	
420	    // If we have more than one reference, decrement and return.
421	    if (entry.value_ptr.ref > 1) {
422	        entry.value_ptr.ref -= 1;
423	        return;
424	    }
425	
426	    // We are at a zero ref count so deinit the group and remove.
427	    entry.key_ptr.deinit();
428	    entry.value_ptr.grid.deinit(self.alloc);
429	    self.alloc.destroy(entry.value_ptr.grid);
430	    self.map.removeByPtr(entry.key_ptr);
431	}
